England squad live: Tuchel explains shock Palmer omission as Jobe Bellingham receives first call-up

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Tuchel explained that Palmer ‘misses too many opportunities to impress’ as he kept faith with his previous squad while offering first-time call-ups to Josh King and Jobe Bellingham

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Thomas Tuchel has left Cole Palmer out his England squad while Jobe Bellingham, the brother of Jude Bellingham, has received his first senior call-up.

Tuchel was critical of Palmer for withdrawing from September’s squad due to injury, with the England manager saying the Chelsea star missed “too many camps with injuries”. Palmer did not miss any games for Chelsea following the international break, but was sent off against Tottenham, and the 24-year-old has now been left out for the Nations League games against Croatia and Spain as the Three Lions aim to book their place in the quarter-finals.

Speaking at a press conference at Wembley, Tuchel explained: “It counts when you are in camp and I just said it last time, he [Palmer] misses too many opportunities to impress. It was a long camp. It was two-and-a-half weeks and the players and the boys who were in camp built something extraordinary throughout these weeks. That's why the credit goes to them. The trust is for them. If you are in camp, this is your chance to impress. So it's not personal.”

Elsewhere, Jobe Bellingham, 21, joins brother Jude in Tuchel’s squad after impressing for Borussia Dortmund this season, while there is also a first-time call-up for Fulham midfielder Josh King, 19. Trent Alexander-Arnold keeps his place despite his lack of minutes for Real Madrid while Arsenal right back Ben White also returns following injury, but there is no place for winger Noni Madueke despite the absence of Bukayo Saka due to injury.

England are third in Group A3 after four games, having won three and lost one, to Spain at Wembley. They host Croatia on Thursday before travelling to Madrid to take on Spain on Sunday, and a point from either of those two games would be enough to qualify for the knockout rounds in March.

Follow all the latest updates from England’s squad announcement and Tuchel’s Wembley press conference below.

From childhood Champions League finals on a patch of grass in Stourbridge to the biggest stage in international football, Jobe Bellingham is determined to step out of his brother’s shadow.

Myles Lewis-Skelly is absent for England’s match against Croatia due to suspension.

Thomas Tuchel addressed this saying: “We have a slight issue in central midfield because Alex Scott is not ready. Unfortunately he picked up an injury in the last camp and can’t be with us.

“Myles, who featured in all four matches, picked up a yellow card and is suspended for the Croatia match and it leaves us a little bit short in central midfield, especially for the first match.

“(Under-21s boss) Lee Carsley has spoken highly of Jobe for weeks and months that if there is a spot and possibility to join and make an impression in the first team.

“He had good matches, and he is on a good run of form in Dortmund, so he deserves to be with us.”

Jobe Bellingham and Josh King have been included in the final England squad of the year, but Thomas Tuchel has overlooked Cole Palmer and Kobbie Mainoo for November’s internationals.

Djed Spence and Ben White were brought into a 25-man squad that defender Nico O’Reilly has dropped out of.

Declan Rice returns after injury but Bukayo Saka is absent with a thigh injury and Tuchel decided not to bring in Noni Madueke as his Arsenal team-mate’s replacement.

There was no place for World Cup squad members Dean Henderson, Dan Burn and Jordan Henderson after missing the last camp.

Alex Scott, Marcus Rashford and John Stones are ruled out of this meet up through injury, as are Tino Livramento, Reece James and Trevoh Chalobah.

England will play the final two matches of their Nations League group stage in this break.

First up is Croatia on Thursday 12 November before a blockbuster clash against Spain on Sunday November 15.

The England boss ended his press conference by answering a question on right-back Trent Alexander-Arnold.

He said: "He was very good in the last camp and in the games he played, and he deserved to come back.

"The competition is on, as always. The door is always open. He competes with Jarell [Quansah] and Ben [White] for the right-back position."

Thomas Tuchel was asked about the inclusion of younger players such as Jobe Bellingham and Josh King and whether that signals a new phase for the team.

"I think we did naturally. We were a little bit forced. I was a bit concerned before the last camp because we missed Dean Henderson, John Stones, Dan Burn, Jordan Henderson, Reece James - players who were really responsible for the attitude and the energy at the World Cup and played a huge part in the standards throughout the camp,” Tuchel explained.

"I was a bit worried that we missed so many of them but the group found a new way. They found new leadership, a new way to engage with each other. We built another camp with a good atmosphere, new voices to set the standards.

"Now we have more choices and I had to give Dan Burn, Jordan Henderson, Dean Henderson difficult phonecalls. I am very aware of what they did for us and they built a very strong foundation on which these players now are benefitting from.

"Naturally, we went forward. But we always want to go forward and embrace change as something positive. It does not mean the door is closed for these kinds of players. But we decided to give this group kind of a second push."

The England manager has opted to ‘trust’ the players who impressed him in the last squad, while also offering first-time call-ups to Josh King and Jobe Bellingham.

On Jobe Bellingham’s call up, Tuchel said: "It's an incredible achievement for the brothers and the family to be both nominated for the national team, which is an incredible story in itself.

“I was very happy to give him the call-up and I saw his excitement straight away in his smile. He is a very open and calm personality so it was a very nice phone call, of course, because it was very good news.

"He is one of five players for the central midfield. He is a more defensive player than his brother Jude. Good on the ball, good physique, very physical player who can keep possession. Now it is up to him to impress."

“Me personally, I’m targeting top spot and I would be very surprised if my group doesn’t.

“This is clearly the target, we want to have a final in Spain because we want to have these pressure moments and these matches when we play for something.

“We want to play under pressure and in the big occasions. We want to use the Nations League for exactly that to be prepared in the best possible way for the Euros.

“For me, Croatia is a crucial match to win especially after the result we got. We got a deserved result [7-0 in Croatia] but it was kind of a freak result that does not reflect the quality of Croatia in general.

“It reflected the way the match went last time but we have to forget that result and remember what we put in and what quality we put in and what effort we top.

“We need another top performance because Croatia showed an excellent response against Spain straight after.”

"I have little a standby list and the clubs and the players know it, players who cover us on a lot of positions,” Tuchel explained.

"But we have 22 outfield players so this should not affect us. It is a short camp and we should be fine number-wise.

"It is always about the ones who are in camp, who don't withdraw and walk the talk, come to camp and show that it matters.

"I think that [message] is naturally coming through. That was always the message very, very early since we took charge that is matters if you are in camp and what you do in camp.

"The last camp was an excellent one and the trust and belief is in the players who were there."

Liebherr expands excavator range with new, 250-tonne electric model

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

It takes some seriously heavy equipment to make an ultra-class mining truck look little, but that’s exactly what German equipment giant Liebherr does best, and its newest, 250-tonne production excavator keeps that tradition alive – only this time, it’s electric.

Liebherr began converting its diesel-powered mining excavators to electric in limited numbers back in 2024, and electric power has proven to be effective – moving millions of tons of dirt while saving millions of dollars in fuel and maintenance costs. Now, the company is translating that testing experience into regular production practice with the launch of a new, all-electric machine. Meet the R 9300 G8-E.

Based on the proven R 9300 G8 platform, the new Liebherr R 9300 G8-E packs an 850 kW (~1,140 hp) electric drivetrain designed to IEC standards and supports 6-6.6 kV power supplies. The company says its electric setup offers customers the same hydraulic power and bucket capacity (1.8 tonnes/cubic meter, or ~1.5 short tons/cubic yard) as the diesel version.

All that electric power ships with advanced electronics and operator assistance systems designed to maximize both efficiency and safety while giving mine operators more data to work and plan with. Liebherr describes its take on ADAS thusly:

The R 9300 G8-E comes with Liebherr Power Efficiency – Hydraulic Control as standard, which adapts hydraulic power to suit the machine’s load profile, reducing overall engine load factor. Thanks to its Generation 8 electronic architecture, the R 9300 G8-E is compatible with a range of Liebherr’s IoMine technology products, including its range of excavator Assistance Systems, the semi-autonomous Bucket Filling Assistant, the Skyview vision system as well as the data visualisation tool, Data View. Customers also have the option of installing Liebherr’s autonomous cable reel solution onto the excavator to assist with cable management and support machine mobility on site.

Like its repowered cousins, the big electric excavator does seem to have on-board batteries, but is primarily driven and operated with grid power, reducing charging and re-charging concerns and enabling round-the-clock operations for as long as grid power holds.

Liebherr touts the benefits of new R 9300 G8-E’s quiet, emissions-free running throughout its official announcement, but doesn’t stop there. The company says its EV, which makes do with thousands of fewer moving parts in its drivetrain, can also reduce maintenance requirements and associated operating costs in certain applications and site conditions while improving workplace conditions for the people who work in and around the machine.

The electric drivetrain also reduces vibration inside the cab, improving operator comfort, limiting fatigue, and potentially contributing to a safer working environment.

Waymo’s co-CEO tells Bloomberg the Uber tie-up was a ‘crapshoot’ for riders

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Uber says robotaxis get about 30% more trips a day on its app and plans more than 2,000 by 2027. AI agents booked just four of its rides in Halloween week.

Waymo co-chief executive Tekedra Mawakana

Waymo co-chief executive Tekedra Mawakana says partnering with Uber was “a little bit of a crapshoot” for riders. On the same day, Uber told investors that robotaxis earn more on its platform than on their makers’ own apps.

Mawakana spoke to Emily Chang on Bloomberg’s The Circuit. She explained why Waymo will end Uber’s exclusive hold on its robotaxis in Austin and Atlanta in January 2028. We reported the plan in July. Riders in those cities can only book a Waymo through Uber. They can ask for a robotaxi, but they get one only if it is nearby.

“In the early days, we would partner because we didn’t have the scale, and in that partnering, it was a little bit of a crapshoot. Will I get a Waymo? Will I not get a Waymo?” Mawakana said.

Riders want one consistent app, she said. That is especially true of those who already use Waymo’s own app in San Francisco. Waymo will launch its app in Austin and Atlanta instead.

“It’s important for us to have that relationship with the rider. We’ve gotten that feedback from the riders in those markets,” she said. “And at the same time, learning and partnering with Uber has been a really important part of our journey.”

An Uber spokesperson told Bloomberg that Uber had asked Waymo to add a robotaxi option in Austin and Atlanta. Waymo said no. Waymo declined to comment on the talks. Uber told TechCrunch it still plans to host Waymo robotaxis for as long as both companies agree.

Uber made its case in the prepared remarks for its third-quarter call. It said its US robotaxi partners get about 30% more trips per vehicle a day on Uber. The comparison is with first-party deployments. In an illustrative example, a 30% rise in paid trips cuts the fixed cost per trip by about 23%. Uber said that leaves “room for higher fleet profitability even after paying Uber’s marketplace fee”.

“As autonomous technology becomes more widely available, we believe this ability to commercialize vehicles profitably will become an increasingly important source of competitive advantage and value capture,” the company wrote.

Uber expects about 700 fully driverless vehicles on its platform by the end of this year. It plans more than 2,000 by the end of 2027, roughly half of them abroad. After that, Uber expects what it calls “a much larger commercial ramp beginning in 2028”.

Chief executive Dara Khosrowshahi told CNBC that Uber has committed about $10bn to driverless partners and investments, including Rivian, Nuro and Avride.

In Las Vegas, Uber expects Amazon’s Zoox to launch on its app later this month, followed by Motional. It plans to add a dedicated autonomous option in the app there “as availability and reliability improve”, its remarks said. Nuro and Lucid robotaxis are due in the Bay Area in the first quarter of 2027.

Waymo runs about 4,000 vehicles and more than 500,000 trips a week across 14 US cities, according to Bloomberg. It plans to grow its fleet fivefold by 2027.

Uber also gave a figure for bookings by AI agents, TechCrunch reported. Chief financial officer Balaji Krishnamurthy said on the call that Uber handled about 320 million trips and orders in the week of Halloween. AI agents placed about 500 of the Uber Eats orders, and Uber said they booked four rides.

“It is very, very early and very limited traction that we are seeing so far,” Khosrowshahi said.

Booking a ride in Uber’s app takes about 50 seconds on average, he said. Visa’s tests found AI models failing as shoppers this week too.

Uber’s third-quarter gross bookings rose 20% to $59.8bn, and revenue rose 10% to $14.8bn. Revenue from rides fell 2% to $7.52bn, while delivery grew 23%. Uber said an accounting change in the UK, which moved driver payments out of its costs, cut $1.1bn from revenue. Khosrowshahi said high fuel prices and fewer drivers held back rides in several international markets. Uber’s shares rose more than 7% on Wednesday, CNBC reported.

Mawakana said Waymo will not compete on price. Its rides still cost more than taxis or ride-hailing on average, Bloomberg reported.

“Price is not the first priority. We are not trying to have this race to the bottom on what we’re offering to our riders. We really want to elevate the experience over time,” she said.

Birmingham City fans will love Jude Bellingham's reaction to England news

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Jude and Jobe Bellingham have received call-ups to the England senior squad under Thomas Tuchel for the first time as a duo, with the former paying tribute to their early-career spells at Birmingham City.

The Premier League and Championship will halt their respective campaigns after the upcoming weekend's round of fixtures, with the Three Lions in Nations League action against Croatia and Spain.

It has been a relatively successful year for England on the footballing stage, with a third-placed finish at the FIFA World Cup hosted in the United States, Canada and Mexico being the nation's best in the tournament since winning it in 1966.

Jude Bellingham was an integral part of the side's run to the semi-finals and has continued to impress in the four Nations League matches during the previous international window.

Many of the high-profile names at Tuchel's disposal can credit spells at various EFL clubs for aiding their eventual development, including Ballon d'Or winner, Harry Kane.

Unsurprisingly, Bellingham was quick to reference his boyhood club yet again in a proud family milestone.

A reunion with Chris Wood has been debated amongst Bluenoses ahead of the January window

Jude and Jobe Bellingham are the most high-profile names to have graduated from Birmingham's Wast Hills academy setup this century, with the former becoming the club's youngest-ever player and goalscorer seven years ago before initially moving to Borussia Dortmund.

The West Midlands outfit then opted to retire the '22' shirt to help create a legacy, and that has only grown stronger through the 23-year-old's current exploits with Real Madrid and England.

Jobe, 21, played 26 times for Blues between 2022 and 2023 before moving to Sunderland for £3m, and after the Black Cats' Premier League return, also signed for Dortmund in a £27.8m deal.

He has since shone for the Bundesliga giants, and after impressing domestically and in the UEFA Champions League, the 12-time Under-21s international earned his first senior Three Lions call-up on Friday.

Such feats were marked by his older brother on social media, who posted a picture of them sporting Birmingham colours during their academy days.

During the early years of his career, Jobe has often been compared to his older brother, despite rightfully wanting to create his own story on the pitch.

That has clearly been the case given his rave reviews at Dortmund, whilst he also racked up over 100 Championship appearances for Birmingham and Sunderland.

Like Jude, the Stourbridge native has never lost sight of the impact Blues staff had in order for him to flourish at such a young age.

“I’m so grateful for everything they’ve (Birmingham) done for me over many years, since I was really young," he said shortly after leaving B9.

“At Birmingham, a lot of the academy coaches helped loads, just with my understanding of the game. I think they’ve made me a better person, I believe. There’s also a lot of players in the first team who helped me grow into the first-team environment.

Whilst they have their own career paths to focus on, both Jude and Jobe will be loving the developments at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park right now, which is a stark contrast to how the club was run during their respective breakthrough years.

Knighthead's plans for the Sports Quarter and Powerhouse stadium have seen Jude involved in promotional videos, whilst he was also present at the pre-season friendly against FC Barcelona.

On the pitch, Chris Davies' side are doing their utmost to end a long-standing Premier League exile, as Blues sit third in the Championship having lost just two of their first 14 games.

Joyous feelings will be reciprocated by those associated with the club for the progress made by the duo, who, in turn, will hope to see the club back in the top-flight very soon.

Are you ok, Noni?

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Arsenal’s squad construction has given them a lot of depth and diversity in terms of profiles. Timber and White for right-back, Calafiori and Hincapie for left-back. Ezri Konsa has, incredibly, offered both protection and threat to the impregnable Saliba and Gabriel partnership. Bruno and Lewis Skelly, Rice and Zubimendi and Odegaard and Merino for midfield.

Eze and Tzolis for the left-wing. Havertz and Gyokeres for centre-forward. Of course, some of these pairings are more symbiotic than others. Mosquera is probably going to fall behind Konsa as a back up to either one of Saliba or Gabriel. In reality, Zubimendi is only going to play ahead of Rice if Rice is injured or Arsenal have a nice easy game against League Two opposition, for example…

On the face of it, Noni Madueke should really be in that third layer of the squad with Mosquera, Kepa and where Zubimendi probably now resides. The role of back-up to Bukayo Saka ought to have been one with limited opportunity. In a sense, I was always a little surprised that Madueke signed up for it when he came to the club from Chelsea last summer.

I was slightly surprised that Arsenal spent so much money on a player who was always incredibly unlikely to dislodge Saka no matter how much he improved. And how much scope was there going to be for Madueke to get the sort of rhythm and consistency of selection to hone his talents?

Now I strongly suspect that Arsenal would have had anxieties over Saka’s ability to stay fit after tearing his hamstring in December 2024 and missing a few months of football. It was the first significant injury Saka had incurred and after quickly deciding that loanee winger Raheem Sterling was not going to be an adequate replacement, they amended for his absence with a blend of 17-year-old Ethan Nwaneri and surplus left-back Kieran Tierney.

It isn’t difficult to see how Arsenal viewed the right-wing spot as a single point of failure but encouraging Madueke to see it as an attractive position must have challenged even Mikel Arteta’s powers of persuasion. Yet just over a year on from signing for Arsenal, Madueke has a league title winners medal (‘while others are tweeting and posting’) with 25 starts to his name last season, 16 of which were in the Premier League.

In reality, being Bukayo Saka’s third leg has, to this point, proved to be a savvy career move for Madueke because he is getting plenty of time on the pitch in Saka’s increasing absences. This extends to the England National Team too, where Madueke plays the same role. But it was a role that still earned him four starts for his country in a World Cup, including a start in the quarter-final against Norway.

A Premier League title winners medal, a Champions League Final appearance and four World Cup starts was not a bad return for Madueke in terms of playing time. I think we knew when Arsenal bought him that Noni was a talented but phlegmatic player, relentless in taking on his full-back but the quality of his end-product could vary markedly.

In his new signing profile of Madueke on Arseblog, Phil Costa wrote, ‘there’s a feeling that Arsenal lack penetration from their wingers (Bukayo Saka aside); chaos factor, some unpredictability, and Madueke brings this in spades. His first thought when receiving the ball is centred around provoking defenders and driving towards goal, which means you can isolate him without too much focus on the overall setup – you just wind him up and let him go.

‘There are two specific metrics to love when assessing the 23-year-old; per FBRef data, he currently sits in the 95th percentile for progressive carries (6.82 per 90) and the 98th percentile for carries into the box (4.12 per 90). Those are big numbers.’ There was never any doubt about Madueke’s ability to get the ball into threatening areas, the question has always been what he does when he gets there.

Last season, some clips of him harumphing the ball out of the stadium on his right foot went semi-viral. I also think some of his performances were impacted by a lack of balance in the front three. With technicians like Havertz and Odegaard unavailable, he was often playing alongside fellow blunderbusses like Gyokeres and Martinelli.

I admit that my tolerance for inefficiency is high when it comes to attackers. I think attacking is a volume game. The vast majority of attacks come to nothing, the vast majority of shots do not go in or even work a goalkeeper. Individual attacks are the weather but, overall, attacking is the climate. The more you do it and sustain it, the more likely you are to score.

So my ‘we can fix him’ gene for slightly wayward wingers is high. I didn’t even mind Gervinho (a frustrating player, no doubt, but the Bradford miss has become the defining image of his Arsenal career which I don’t believe was fully representative of his performances).

The video of Madueke’s right foot ballooning the ball into the upper tier against Kairat last season has been refined. Both his goals this season have been on his right foot, particularly notably against Nottingham Forest where he cut in on his right and unleashed a low and controlled shot. It might have taken a helpful deflection but that’s because he kept the attempt low.

Last season, his right footed efforts were more in danger of colliding with low flying aircraft on their way into orbit. Noni’s goal against Slavia Praha demonstrated his pace in behind. It was the sort of goal and chance Gabriel Martinelli was hoovering up in Europe last season. European games tend to offer more space in behind and with Martinelli gone, Madueke is one of the few Arsenal attackers explosive enough to exploit it.

Indeed, in my view, he ought to have won Arsenal a match winning penalty in Budapest against Paris Saint Germain in May. There are signs of greater maturity and efficiency in his game. According to WhoScored.com his pass success rate in the Premier League last season was 76.2%. This season so far it is 86.3%.

I think he has learned that he does not have to go for the full-back at 100mph every single time he gets the ball and has varied his game. In the Champions League game against Lille, he sent 15 crosses into the penalty area, having sent six into the area in the game before against Leeds. 15 crosses sounds like a lot but it was very clearly under instruction from Mikel Arteta.

After that Lille game, the manager said of Madueke, ‘I said when you talk about players being ‘clinical’, sometimes that means scoring five in every 100. It’s about generating volume, more actions and getting him into the good positions, because he will decide matches. He had a really big contribution a few days ago, today he had a contribution in the last final pass as well.’

Being omitted from the England squad in September, according to Arteta, provided additional motivation for Noni, ‘After the break, he is showing something else, another level of anger, another level of conviction as well as in his actions and he is becoming decisive in matches, which is what we expect from a player like him.’

He has now been omitted from the England squad again for the November international round. It is a decision I don’t really understand from Thomas Tuchel but I am happy if it continues to provide Madueke with an additional reason to fine tune his game and because it hopefully protects a player who has become very important.

I admit I am not entirely sure how good Madueke is going to be in, say, three years’ time. He is 24 and it’s at that age that attackers typically start to increase their decisive actions. He has Max Dowman in the rearview mirror too. I think Madueke has a really high ceiling and there are small, but encouraging, signs of improvement.

ANC faces bruising defeat in South African local elections with 80% of ballots counted

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Former liberation movement’s share of national vote almost 14 percentage points below 2021 result

The African National Congress is on course to suffer a bruising defeat in South Africa’s local elections, with the once dominant party of Nelson Mandela falling to under a third of the vote nationally with more than 80% of ballots counted.

Wednesday’s fragmented vote marks another stage in the decline of the ANC, which has led national governments since the end of white minority apartheid rule in 1994. High unemployment, crime, corruption and poor local service delivery have all contributed to voters turning away from the party.

The former liberation movement is projected to lose its status as the biggest party in the two most populous provinces, Gauteng and KwaZulu-Natal, as parties began coalition negotiations behind closed doors. The final results are expected to be announced on Sunday.

The ANC was on 31.8% of the vote nationally, almost 14 percentage points below its 2021 local election result. The centre-right, white-led Democratic Alliance (DA) had 24.1% of the vote, while the uMkhonto weSizwe (MK) party of the former ANC president Jacob Zuma was on 9.3%.

Turnout also rose in Wednesday’s poll. The election commission estimated that 54.5% of registered voters cast their ballot, up from 46% five years ago.

The final shape of coalitions in some of the country’s biggest cities remained unclear with the final votes still being counted. Senior figures in key parties said they would talk with almost anyone, while keeping negotiating strategies under wraps.

In Johannesburg, South Africa’s commercial capital and biggest city, with 4.8 million people, voters have had to contend with regular water and power cuts, uncollected rubbish and potholes. The municipality, which has seen 10 changes of mayor in the last decade amid unstable coalitions, has admitted to a 220bn rand (£9.9bn) infrastructure spending backlog.

With just over half of votes counted in Johannesburg, the DA was leading with 40%, having run an energetic campaign under Helen Zille, a 75-year-old former Cape Town mayor. The ANC, which leads the current coalition, was in second place on 17.5%. ActionSA, a rightwing party led by Herman Mashaba, was on 15.5%. Mashaba has pledged never to work with Zille, whom he calls “racist”, something she vehemently denies.

Many of the ANC’s township strongholds have yet to report full results. The Council for Scientific and Industrial Research (CSIR), a respected thinktank, has projected the DA will finish with 34%, the ANC 20% and ActionSA 16.8%.

Coalitions in Gauteng’s three city municipalities are often negotiated together. The DA is leading in all three.

The MK party, led by 84-year-old Zuma as he continues to weather and deny numerous corruption allegations, is expected to displace the ANC as the largest party in KwaZulu-Natal. Founded in 2023, it mixes socially conservative Zulu nationalism with pledges to redistribute land and wealth from white to black South Africans.

With half of votes counted in eThekwini municipality, which contains Durban, the MK had 41%, meaning other parties could form a coalition to keep it out of power. Meanwhile, the DA is projected to maintain its majority in Cape Town, despite growing middle-class anger over rising housing costs.

Voters choose a local councillor and a party, with seats allocated via proportional representation. Councillors will then have two weeks to elect mayors and form municipal governments.

Android is getting my favorite iOS tracking feature – what to expect

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Tech > Services & Software > Operating Systems > Mobile OS > Android

'Notify when left behind' is making its way to Android - but to limited devices for now.

A much-needed update — “Notify when left behind” — is rolling out to Google’s Find My hub. The feature sends a notification when a registered device is separated from your phone, except when you leave it at your house.

Also: 7 Galaxy settings I always change to boost my battery life

According to 9to5Google, this feature was first previewed in October, with the notification available for Chipolo trackers and Moto Tag.

This feature is not yet available for other peripherals such as headphones. I tried to see if my Sony WH-1000XM6 headphones or Google Pixel Buds Pro 2 have it — they don’t. Hopefully, support is on the way. In the meantime, here’s what we know.

“Notify when left behind” is my all-time favorite device tracking feature on iOS. Any device set up on my Find My network is compatible with this feature, including my MacBook Air, AirPods Pro 3, and Apple Watch, three devices I often take out of the house — and have indeed left behind once or twice.

Also: Google’s Pixel Tag tracker has a big advantage over AirTags

If these devices are separated from my phone in an unsaved location, I’ll get a notification of which device I left and where. In iOS, you can customize which locations are “safe,” and won’t notify you if you leave them there.

On Android, this feature is currently limited to only notifying you when a device is left at a location other than the home address registered to your Google account. Hopefully, location customization is on the horizon.

Right now, this feature appears to be limited to specific Bluetooth trackers. Photos shared by 9to5Google show compatible trackers have a “Notify when left behind” tab at the bottom of their menu in the Find Hub, so it’s expected that future supported devices will operate the same way.

9to5Google reports that previous updates to Google Play Services indicated this feature’s existence. According to 9to5Google, “Notify when left behind” should be a feature that users can enable when registering a new device with their Find Hub.

Also: I’m an Apple Maps fan, but I switch to Google Maps for these 3 tasks

Once Google matures and fully rolls out this feature, there should be a setting inside the Find Hub to enable these notifications on every device in your hub.

Android’s breadth of compatibility with third-party manufacturers is its most significant advantage over Apple. Regarding device tracking, you can add Sony headphones, Motorola trackers, and Samsung Galaxy smartwatches to the same Find Hub.

Also: Apple has a Bluetooth problem, and Android is taking advantage

However, my Beats headphones that appear in my Find My hub are not currently supported by this new feature. If Google can expand this feature to all Fast Pair-compatible devices, such as Bluetooth trackers, headphones, smartwatches, laptops, and more, it’ll be easier for consumers to keep tabs on their ever-growing armory of devices.

Home Office cloud services contract may net AWS £1B

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Planning notice puts renewal at nearly twice the 2023 value, just as Whitehall frets about US tech dependence

Amazon Web Services (AWS) looks set to win more than a billion pounds of UK taxpayers' money under a new Home Office contract for public cloud hosting.

A planning notice, published on October 30, covers the renewal of the department's public cloud services, to meet its current and future hosting needs. The estimated value is £881,621,007 ($1.18 billion), which passes £1 billion once VAT is included.

AWS won the existing contract with the Home Office in 2023 for about £450 million (c $603 million). The Register has asked the Home Office why the figure has risen so much, and will update this article if it responds.

Once awarded, the contract is expected to run from December 2027 to December 2029, with a possible extension to December 2031 (four years in total). A market engagement event for suppliers on the G-Cloud 15 Lot 1A procurement framework is scheduled for December 8, giving bidders the chance to pitch.

The Home Office would not say who else is being considered, saying it does not comment on ongoing procurement.

The notice lands as digital sovereignty becomes a bigger problem in the UK and across Europe, amid suspicion that US authorities have cut off access to American technology for political reasons. A survey found that about three-quarters of businesses in the UK, France, and Germany fear Washington could abruptly sever access to the US tech platforms they rely on.

Earlier this year, the Open Rights Group warned politicians that the UK is far too dependent on US technology companies to run critical operations.

Whitehall is mindful of its over-reliance on American hyperscalers. As The Register revealed in 2024, the Cabinet Office admitted - in an internal document seen by us - that the government’s negotiating power had been inhibited by vendor lock-in.

It said the "UK government’s current approach to cloud adoption and management across its departments faces several challenges" which combined result "in risk concentration and vendor lock-in that inhibit UK government’s negotiating power over the cloud vendors." It acknowledged "the existing dominance of AWS and Azure in the UK Government’s cloud services is set to continue."

A source at one supplier that works with the public sector said the UK administration was "failing to support UK [cloud] providers, it says they can’t meet scale and that is garbage." They claimed that in many cases the government people writing the tenders are advised by professional services companies to favor the hyperscalers. The professional services companies are then "rewarded" as migration or integration partners, our source claimed.

The UK government also pledged to steer more business to  small-to-medium sized enterprises (SMEs) in an action plan published last month. In August it added smaller, local suppliers to G-Cloud 15, though a place on the framework does not guarantee any business.

Pledges like this are nothing new, and they have largely come to nothing, as we reported in 2005, 2013, 2016, and 2018.

According to public sector intelligence platform BidSkim, AWS takes 75 percent of the government money on cloud contracts, worth about £1.22 billion ($1.6 billion). It estimates that Microsoft Azure has more contracts, but these are smaller and add up to £355 million ($475 million), while Google Cloud is on £47 million ($63 million) and Oracle Cloud $15 million ($20 million).

Scientists detect ‘pregnant’ coral using ultrasound device

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 6, 2026 12:47 PM EST

Scientists have a new tool in the fight to save Earth’s endangered coral reefs and it’s something many moms-to-be know all too well: the ultrasound.

This week, veteran camera-maker Canon announced its high-tech ultrasound platform can scan living coral submerged underwater and detect eggs developing inside them. Until now, checking whether coral was carrying eggs typically required cutting it open. This ultrasound development means researchers can assess coral reproduction non-invasively for the first time. By using ultrasound, scientists can gather crucial information about a coral’s reproductive health and try to predict when it will release its eggs. That insight can help conservationists time and target their restoration efforts without damaging or sacrificing the species in the process.

Canon conducted the experiment in collaboration with United Kingdom-based conservation organization Coral Spawning International (CSI). The team used Canon’s Aplio i700, an advanced medical ultrasound system normally used on humans in hospital settings. In this case though, it scanned six species of large polyp stony coral with varying skeletal structures. The device’s powerful mixture of high and ultra high frequency probes let researchers see through the coal’s skeletons and view their internal structures. The team verified their results by cutting open one coral species, Trachyphyllia geoffroyi, and taking high powered images of its interior. Those images were then cross referenced with the results from the ultrasound, confirming that the structure identified in the scan were indeed eggs.

“This experiment demonstrates that imaging innovation has the power to show us what was previously unseen, creating a deeper understanding of the world around us,” Canon EMEA Sustainability and Government Affairs Director Peter Bragg said in a statement. “By unlocking this knowledge, we hold a fundamental belief that imaging has the power to transform the world.”

Coal can use all the help it can get. Late last month, the International Union for Conservation of Nature (IUCN) released a sobering assessment warning that nearly all (96 percent) of the Earth’s coral reef ecosystem face an extremely high risk of collapse within the next 50 years. The main culprit? Warming oceans and heat waves brought on by climate change.

That dire threat isn’t just a problem for coral, either. Coral reefs, sometimes referred to as the “rainforests of the sea” are the among the most biodiverse eco systems on the planet and support an estimated quarter of all marine species. When reefs collapse, the coral dies and is replaced by algae and sponges which are not able to support as diverse a range of marine life. As a result, the ocean’s overall biological health ebbs and flows alongside coral.

Understanding when corals will spawn can give scientists critical data for breeding and a leg up on restoration efforts, which can help them try and tip the odds in  coral’s favor. Ultrasound scans could make that work more precise while sparing the coral from an untimely dissection.

Thomas Tuchel hands Jobe Bellingham the chance to carve his own path as an England star

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

From childhood Champions League finals on a patch of grass in Stourbridge to the biggest stage in international football, Jobe Bellingham is determined to step out of his brother’s shadow

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Jobe Bellingham’s football career is strikingly similar to that of his big brother Jude, from the unusual Birmingham-to-Dortmund route right down to their distinctive rangey stride, and so far it has been a case of, anything you can do, I did it faster, better, with a little more panache. But there is one thing Jude didn’t tick off on his meteoric rise – he never captained England Under-21s.

Proudly wearing the armband in September, Jobe shone in a 4-2 comeback win over Kazakhstan in qualifying for next year’s U21 Euros. It was another sign that the 21-year-old’s development is rapidly accelerating.

“He was outstanding,” U21s manager Lee Carsley said. “I went to watch Jobe a couple of weeks ago in Dortmund and he’s progressing so quickly. Physically he’s grown. He’s definitely got that strength in his legs. He’s moving the ball so well and his footwork has really improved. The way he’s moving the ball, taking opponents on, and using his strength and balance … he was always a good player but now he’s going through the levels.”

That, Thomas Tuchel stressed in his Wembley press conference on Friday, was the primary reason for Jobe’s first call-up to the senior England squad, a landmark earned “on his own merit”. The Bellingham brothers are both included in the 25-man group to face Croatia here on Thursday and Spain in Madrid on Sunday, as England – who have virtually qualified for the Nations League quarter-finals in March – go all out to win Group A3.

“Lee is speaking very, very highly of him,” Tuchel explained. “In the Under-21s, he’s a standout player since a long time now. He’s the captain. So I think the door should always be open for that pathway, for special performances over a long period of time, and that’s what Jobe has done.

“He has also found his rhythm with Dortmund, he’s had an excellent start to the season. He’s a regular starter now there and very influential. So there’s an opportunity coming on his own merit. Jobe deserves to make an impression and that’s why we’re glad to have him in camp and see what he can do for us.”

Jobe wears his first name on the back of his shirt, partly in an effort to distinguish himself from his brother, now a fully fledged galactico at Real Madrid who shone at the 2026 World Cup and already has 60 international caps to his name at 23.

“When I signed for Sunderland it was suggested to me by someone, a special coach I had there,” Jobe explained. “I was still young and thought it was cool to have my own name on the back. It didn’t have any deeper meaning but it’s become something unique. It shows that I’m going my own way. Some people don’t like it – I don’t care.”

The comparisons, of course, are inevitable. Both brothers made their debuts for Birmingham City aged 16 before moving to Dortmund – Jude aged 17 in a £25m transfer, Jobe via a successful two-season stint at Sunderland where he won Championship Young Player of the Season, later moving to the German club aged 19 for £28m.

Anyone would think Jobe might choose a different club in order to carve his own path, but he recognised that it would be damaging to make career choices simply to avoid his brother’s footprints, even if he had “fears” before joining Dortmund, where Jude grew into a global star. “It’s not about being different,” Jobe said. “It’s about doing the right thing.”

The brothers are close. They spent every possible minute of their childhood playing football on a small square of grass outside the family home in Stourbridge, up until it got dark. “Every game we played, we always said it was the Champions League final,” said Jude in 2022, two years before winning the actual Champions League final with Real Madrid. “We’d make up teams and make up stadiums that we’d play in. We must have played about 1,000 Champions League finals on that little patch of grass.”

They are both fast, strong and physically imposing central midfielders, standing 6ft plenty, with huge technical ability, coached into them by their father Mark who had a nomadic career as a prolific striker in non-league football. Incidentally, Mark Bellingham has been starring for England recently too, after scoring seven goals on his debut for the England Over-50s Veterans in a 13-1 win against the Republic of Ireland.

But there are also differences in how they play the game. Jude’s strengths are in an advanced No 8 or No 10 role, surging forwards with the ball at his feet, gliding past tacklers, holding off markers and impacting the game with goals. Jobe is more of a traditional box-to-box midfielder with an excellent reading of the game, someone who sits a little deeper and can lay a platform for more attacking teammates.

There is another difference. Jobe is an altogether more low-key figure, and has said he does not wish to “be a superstar... I don’t want to be a big character away from the pitch. When I play I just want to be one of the highest performing players and get my head down.”

Jobe now has the opportunity to begin his own journey as an England international. He will do so not as Jude’s younger brother, but as a midfielder who has earned his chance. Though if he does make his senior debut, Jobe might just happen to have his biggest fan beside him. “As long as he’s happy, that’s all I really care about,” Jude said recently. “His happiness means more to me than my own.”

Goalkeepers: Jordan Pickford, Jason Steele, James Trafford.

Defenders: Jarrad Branthwaite, Marc Guehi, Lewis Hall, Ezri Konsa, Jarell Quansah, Djed Spence, Trent Alexander-Arnold, Ben White.

Midfielders: Elliot Anderson, Jobe Bellingham, Jude Bellingham, James Garner, Josh King, Myles Lewis-Skelly, Declan Rice.

Forwards: Harry Kane, Dominic Calvert-Lewin, Eberechi Eze, Morgan Gibbs-White, Anthony Gordon, Rio Ngumoha, Morgan Rogers

Crystanbul revisited: The night Liverpool’s title hopes spectacularly fell apart

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

When you think back to Liverpool’s stunning collapse to surrender the 2013-14 Premier League title, chances are you remember the Steven Gerrard slip.

That came with three games to go. Chelsea were the visitors and Gerrard’s untimely loss of footing allowed Demba Ba to run through and score.

The west London side would go on to win the game 2-0, with Jose Mourinho banging the Chelsea badge on his blue gilet in front of the away fans.

To mark the Year of the Horse, we tell Chelsea-related tales from previous such cycles in the Lunar New Year… 👀🐎 https://t.co/cxtAKVaSqV

— Chelsea FC (@ChelseaFC) February 17, 2026

But if the loss to Chelsea was the dagger in the heart of Liverpool’s title bid, the next game was the decapitation.

At a time when cooler heads were struggling to prevail, it was hard to see Liverpool’s loss to Chelsea as anything but gift-wrapping the Premier League trophy to Manchester City.

In reality, Liverpool still had a decent chance of winning their first league title in 24 years.

After the Chelsea game, Liverpool had dropped from top spot but only on goal difference with City now leading. The two teams had kept a similar scoring pacing, both nearing 100, but City’s defence had been stronger, giving them a 12-goal difference over their Merseyside opponents.

The path to victory for Liverpool then was to win both games and hope City dropped points or to win by such a margin that they swing the goal difference in their favour.

It wasn’t an unrealistic prospect either, City did have the harder fixtures. While they had two home games, they faced two teams in Aston Villa and West Ham that were yet to be mathematically clear of relegation.

Liverpool meanwhile knew that if they got through the game against Palace, they would play the final match of the season at Anfield against a Newcastle side that was safe in ninth but had a minus 18 goal difference, the worst in the top half of the table.

If given the choice, Brendan Rodgers may well have chosen Palace as the team he fancied to face at this point of the season. They were 11th and already confirmed their place in the Premier League next season. Selhurst Park is about 40 miles away from the coast but Palace should already be on the beach.

Palace were on a good run of form, winning their previous five out of six, but surely, surely Liverpool would comfortably knock them over.

If there was ever a man born to ruin a party, it was Pulis. After the Chelsea defeat, Rodgers complained about the defensive tactics deployed by Mourinho. Pulis probably sat in his wingback armchair at home and a smile appeared across his lips.

The fixture had been selected for the Monday night, meaning Steven Gerrard led his Liverpool team out into the setting South London sun with a point to prove.

The initial focus was on Gerrard. How would the man who was as Liverpool as the Beatles and turning K into Ch when speaking recover from being the man who may have cost them the Premier League title?

That answer was given very quickly. In front of a Crystal Palace stand full of fans that were very clearly up for it, Gerrard whipped in a corner to an unmarked Joe Allen at the back post. The smallest man on the pitch scored against a defence that had not conceded a set-piece goal all season.

The first half ran out as exactly as Liverpool would have hoped. They had 68% of the ball, 12 shots to Palace’s three and were in complete control, much to Pulis’ annoyance.

He told the BBC later that year that he gave his centre-halves Scott Dann and Damien Delaney a b*llocking for not getting close enough to Luis Suarez and Daniel Sturridge, which is easier said than done.

The words of wisdom appeared to go in one ear and out the other as Liverpool scored two quick goals to begin the second half.

Sterling skipped away from the pressure of Joel Ward while both Dann and Delaney had dropped deep to cover the run of Suarez.

The two got closer to tackling each other as Sturridge’s effort deflected off Delaney’s trailing foot and past a helpless Julian Speroni.

Remembering his manager’s half-time words, Delaney soon charges out to try and close down Suarez just past the centre circle in Palace’s half.

His horror show of a second half continues as he instead controls it for Suarez before being completely wrong-footed as the Uruguayan charges at the two Palace defenders now left.

Raheem Sterling was on hand to relieve the pass and pull Dann away from Suarez and even with the English winger’s pass being completely underhit, Dann misses it and Suarez gets it through Speroni’s legs on the way to the net.

Liverpool smelt blood, but in many ways the quick nature of their two goals proved to be their demise.

Rodgers revealed the message at half-time was just to win and that “if it’s 1-0, it’s 1-0.” but asking for calm heads at this time was too much for a team that had been feeding off euphoria.

Ahead of the Chelsea game, Liverpool had won their last 10 Premier League games. They had led the league for the past four weeks and 24 years of pent-up frustration had morphed into a feeling of this finally being the year, of it being destiny. For a long while, Rodgers and the players harnessed that feeling.

This was not just any team either, for you would be hard-pressed to find another city in England where football is more woven into the fabric than it is in Liverpool. They had suffered at the hands of Manchester United and now they needed it to be their turn.

That pandemonium was going through the players’ minds when Suarez raced to grab the ball at 3-0.

It was a powder keg ready to explode and the Selhurst Park faithful needed little reason to believe. The spark that set the whole thing ablaze came from the man who had helped gift Liverpool two goals, Damien Delaney.

In pushing forward for more goals, Liverpool had left themselves open at the back. The midfield pair of Gerrard and Lucas were particularly at fault for allowing Delaney to reach just outside the box completely unmarked.

The back four had all dropped deep, prompting Glen Johnson to neither go with his fellow defenders or close down Delaney. Instead, he stood stiff, waited for Delaney to shoot and turned his body sideways in order to protect himself.

Having been the victim of a deflection earlier in the half, Delaney was now the beneficiary of one. The ball came off the stomach of Johnson and went straight into the top left corner.

At the time, Martin Tyler, who was on commentary for Sky, said it would “get the goal difference back for Liverpool” with the possibility of something even worse not yet entering his mind.

Right then, Rodgers should have reacted. He had two experienced centre-backs in Daniel Agger and Kolo Toure sitting on the bench but they remained uncalled for as Liverpool pushed forward once again.

Just a minute later, Liverpool had seven men in the opposition box when Palace’s Yannick Bolasie skipped past the half-hearted tackle from Philippe Coutinho. Johnson again adopted the ‘stand still and hope I get him’ approach, which Bolasie said ‘thank you very much to’ as he knocked it by him.

The Liverpool players did get back but to nowhere useful. Dwight Gayle gave Jon Flanagan a shoulder feint, meaning he had plenty of room to poke home Bolasie’s pass.

You could barely hear the stadium announcer announcing the goal over the sound of the crowd. Palace was up for it. Selhurst Park was up for it. Liverpool were collapsing under the fear of their own nightmare.

This time Rodgers did react, but he only took off Sturridge for Victor Moses who at this point in his career was still a winger.

The midfield two of Lucas and Gerrard were completely bypassed once again as a chipped ball was controlled by the chest of Glenn Murray.

Martin Skrtel had come out to try and get close to Murray, leaving a gaping hole for Gayle. He waited for the ball to bounce before slotting it past Mignolet. The Liverpool collapse was complete.

“Liverpool have caved in,” shouted Tyler. Rodgers had a blank expression on his face, masking what must have been absolute despair on the inside.

The final whistle blew soon after. Liverpool, whose modern history had been built on the overturning of a 3-0 deficit, had just been on the wrong side of that very moment.

Gerrard was on his haunches. Suarez cried into his shirt as he bent over. With that, Liverpool’s title hopes were over.

“There is no doubt Manchester City will go on and win it,” said Rodgers. “We needed to win to keep the pressure on going into the last game and we did not do that.”

Winning the Premier League title would have been the apex of Rodgers’ career. He would have had a figure as beloved as Klopp. Maybe they could have kept hold of Suarez for another year and made a push in the Champions League.

That night in South London completely changed the trajectory of Liverpool Football Club. The always next year team would have to wait another year at least. In the end, they had to wait another six.

Since that night, Liverpool have won 10 times at Selhurst Park, including a 7-0 thumping, and yet Selhurst Park will always be a painful memory.

Nine minutes of madness had defined a whole season in one of the greatest Premier League moments of all time.

Review: KLM 787-10 Business Class (Amsterdam – Los Angeles), Still A Solid Choice

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

I don’t need an airline to surprise me every time. Sometimes I want to know what I’m getting. My KLM 787-10 business class flight from Amsterdam to Los Angeles reminded me why I keep choosing KLM…and why I still wish it would turn down the heat!

I recently flew KLM’s Boeing 787-10 in World Business Class from Amsterdam to Los Angeles once again, about a year after the flight reviewed below. The seat and overall experience have changed little, so I’ll offer a more abbreviated review of my latest flight. My comprehensive review follows, with a detailed look at the cabin and seat features. In short: nothing has changed at KLM, for better or for worse.

Yes, especially for 60,000 Flying Blue miles (though I’d probably now pay the extra to get out of “light” class). The seat was comfortable and the food was excellent. I enjoyed the flight even though I knew almost exactly what to expect.

The warm cabin remains my biggest complaint, particularly because I encountered the same issue on this route last year. Individual air vents would help accommodate those of us who prefer a cooler sleeping environment.

Even so, this was a comforting way to get home to Los Angeles. Familiar and even boring does not have to mean disappointing.

The detailed review below covers my April 2025 flight on the same aircraft type and route. It includes more detailed info on the seat features, inflight entertainment, Wi-Fi, lavatories, and amenity kit.

My 11-hour KLM flight from Amsterdam to Los Angeles on the 787-10 in World Business Class was smooth, comfortable, but a little warm…

I’ve reviewed the KLM A330, 777, and 787-9 in business class and also the 787-10 in economy class, but this was my first time flying the 787-10 in business class.

I arrived at the KLM Crown Lounge quite early to get my work done for the day, just in case the internet did not function on my flight. While the lounge gets crowded, I quite like it.

Boarding began at 9:05, 45 minutes before departure.

I entered via door 2L and proceeded through the galley and turned left to my seat in 4K.

The KLM 787-10 business class cabin features 38 Jamco America Venture seats arranged in a 1-2-1 reverse herringbone configuration. These Japanese-made seats are 10-15% lighter than traditional reverse herringbone seats.

Seat pitch is generous and seats convert into a fully lie-flat bed (75 inches long). There’s a side storage compartment, seat controls on the armrest, two USB-A ports (one on the side and one under the screen), and a universal power outlet.

My left armrest could be raised (for use when seated) and lowered (to give more space for sleeping).

Interestingly, I noticed the seat looked a bit tattered…at least cosmetically. The paint was chipped on the armrest and under the monitor and the (faux) leather shell around the seat was discolored. There was also noticeable dirt and grime. Everything functioned, but it really looked like an old product, even though my aircraft, PH-BKG, was only delivered five years ago.

Bedding on KLM is comparatively average, with a large pillow and soft duvet-style blanket. There is no day blanket or mattress pad.

I tried to sleep after lunch but the cabin was warm and there were no personal air vents. I did manage a few hours of sleep, but I would have loved to sleep eight hours…a cool cabin and a plush mattress pad would have helped.

The retrofitted 777 World Business Class cabins offer suites with closing doors; the 787-10 do not have this extra feature.

The 11-hour journey to California included lunch after takeoff, a midflight snack, and a meal before landing. Menus were waiting on each seat and KLM continues to print menus for each flight (you’ll notice on the backside of the menu lists your flight number and flight date).

Pre-departure beverages were offered before takeoff, including beer, sparkling wine, orange juice, and “Royal Blue Legend,” the KLM signature cocktail with lemon, ginger, and spirulina…which I had to try.

Lunch began after takeoff, with a refreshing amuse bouche (I don’t even know what it was, but the green thing was topped in a toasted walnut and was very nice) and a drink (I enjoyed “The Very High Fashioned” cocktail, a riff on an Old Fashioned).

I had chili shrimps with pickled watermelon and beetroot mousse and fresh watercress for my appetizer…a great collection of flavors. I was served with a small green salad and bread with Dutch butter. I switched to a Boschendal Boschen Blane 2023 (white wine from South Africa).

For my main course, I chose salmon à la Flamande with Hollandaise sauce, served with white asparagus, scrambled eggs, and potatoes. The salmon filet was very small, but it was a good choice.

For dessert, cheese (blue cheese and Beemster Royaal Fenugreek cheese) with crackers, grapes, and a raisin salad with pine nuts, followed by a tart raspberry mousse topped with sunflower seeds, topped with passion fruit crumble.

About halfway through the flight, passengers were offered a vegetarian croquette sandwich with mustard (I tried that) or a mini club sandwich. Both were served with cherry cheesecake ice cream, which I skipped.

A self-serve station was also set up in the galley with Dutch snacks:

105 minutes before landing, a Caesar salad with pulled chicken, egg, tomatoes, Reypenaar cheese, and pine nuts was offered along with a side of pumpkin ravioli in cream artichoke sauce. A very nice pre-arrival meal, though the pasta was served wrapped in aluminum foil…that could have easily been removed.

The internet worked well on the flight (the flight pass was €22) and it turned out to be a very productive flight when it became clear I could not sleep.

I also managed one movie on the flight, an old Brad Pitt, Kevin Spacey, and Morgan Freeman film called Seven. A little jarring, to say the least…

The KLM IFE library includes music, movies, TV shows, games, and a moving flight map.

KLM’s 787-10 has two lavatories in the front of the cabin reserved for business class passengers (and also for crew use). As always on KLM, I appreciate the tulip flower and the Delft Blue House wallpaper. Soap products were from Marie-Stella-Maris.

The amenity kit also had products from Marie-Stella-Maris (face cream and lip balm) and also included socks, a pen, earplugs, and a dental kit (with tootpaste tablets instead of a tube of toothpaste).

As we approached LAX, the purser came around with Delft Blue Houses, the signature “souvenir” of KLM World Business Class. I was hoping for house 98, which was the parental home of Anthony Fokker in Haarlem (Fokker went on to become one of the world’s greatest aircraft builder in the late 1920’s with factories in the Netherlands and the USA) and I got it!

Then the lady in front of me did not want a house, so I asked if I could have hers and got a second one, 89.

The crew was very seasoned/mature (as always on KLM, it seems) and also very lovely. Very attentive service with a smile and I appreciated so much that flight attendants roamed the cabin throughout the flight, not just during the formal meal services.

It was a beautiful morning (yes, still morning) when we landed at LAX 11 hours later and we flew right over downtown LA and the iconic In-N-Out next to LAX.

KLM offers a very average business class, which is to say a very solid business class. I enjoyed good service, great food, and a comfortable seat on the flight, though the lack of air vent and warm cabin made sleeping difficult. I like the 787-10 and look forward to flying KLM again.

Arsenal handed double boost as defender spotted in training after Mikel Arteta update

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Arsenal, featuring team news and expert analysis from Matt Verri

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Ezri Konsa took part in Arsenal training ahead of the Premier League clash against Hull, with injury fears allayed after also being named in the England squad.

Konsa had emerged as a doubt for Saturday’s game at the Emirates Stadium after being forced off against Slavia Prague earlier in the week with “some issues”, as revealed by Gunners manager Mikel Arteta.

In the first half of the Champions League tie, Konsa was seen feeling his knee and it was decided no risks would be taken with the summer signing from Aston Villa as he made way for Cristhian Mosquera just after the hour mark.

"Obviously he's playing a lot," Arteta said. "He's played so many games in the last few weeks. He had some issues and we decided to take him off."

There were encouraging signs regarding Konsa’s fitness upon being called into Thomas Tuchel’s England squad for the Nations League double-header against Croatia and Spain later this month.

Furthermore, the defender was spotted in Friday’s training session which suggests he will be available for selection as Arsenal look to beat Hull and maintain their three-point lead at the Premier League summit heading into the international break.

Arteta will provide a further update on Konsa in the pre-match press conference, though it would appear the Gunners boss has a near fully-fit squad to choose from.

William Saliba also trained as he pushes to make his first appearance of the season having made a full recovery from a back injury that curtailed his World Cup with France in the summer.

"He's in a really good place," Arteta said on Tuesday. "The process and the timings and the manner that the injury has been dealt with, especially the feelings that Willy has been having along the way, they have been very, very positive.

"His evolution has been as expected, which is not always the case. He feels really comfortable.

"He played 45 minutes behind closed doors last week. He feels ready to go. Whether we use him or not now is a matter of what the team needs."

Netflix’s Animated Twilight Adaptation Confirmed for Two Seasons

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Love it or hate it, you've probably encountered the Twilight series at some point in your life. And the popularity of the vampire romantic drama is not over yet: Netflix is taking Midnight Sun, a 2020 novel that retells Twilight from Edward Cullen's perspective, and turning it into an animated series. It is now confirmed that the Midnight Sun adaptation will be a two-season series.

Twilight is a fantasy movie starring Robert Pattinson and Kristen Stewart, and it took the world by storm in 2008. It is an adaptation of Stephenie Meyer's 2005 vampire romance book. Despite being heavily parodied at the time, it had a following of millions. For many, it was the best thing since sliced bread. Whether you fall into that category or not, you might be interested in Netflix's animated series adaptation.

Netflix is dipping its toes into the Twilight saga with the release of an animated show called Midnight Sun, based on the 2020 novel by Stephenie Meyer. While the original Twilight book is narrated by Bella Swan, Midnight Sun goes through the same events but from Edward Cullen's point of view.

Rather than seeing Bella figure out who and what Edward is, Midnight Sun lets readers understand what goes through his mind as a vampire navigating the world and as he falls in love with Bella. It's an interesting perspective, and we get first-hand experience of what he was thinking at key points in the story — like the biology classroom scene — which is completely different compared to Bella's point of view.

Despite Midnight Sun being only one book, Netflix is extending the adaptation into a two-season series. Actors were being actively cast for the series this summer, and reports indicate that production was scheduled to take place between August 2026 and May 2027. Beyond that, not much has been revealed, which isn't surprising since Twilight fans will know most of the plot details already.

Midnight Sun might sound like a strange pick for an adaptation, but the popularity of the franchise hasn't waned yet. Whether it's because of nostalgia or genuine love for the story and characters, the Twilight series is still able to rise to the top of the charts nearly 20 years later.

Fans are eager to return to the gloomy town of Forks, Washington, but the series is not coming out just yet. Netflix's Midnight Sun is currently in development, and it does not have a release window at the time of writing.

Steam gets a boatload of restored Wing Commanders and Ultima ports after EA producer stumbles across cupboard of old tapes

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

"He took me over there, opened it up and it was like Indiana Jones"

EA have stuck a bunch of restored versions and ports of classic games, on Steam, GOG and the EA App. 25 games in all, including multiple entries in the Wing Commander and Ultima series, are part of the haul. Why's this happening now? Well, an EA producer stumbled across a cupboard full of old tapes, film reels and CDs.

According to an interview with The Game Business, the discovery of this old gubbins was a consequence of EA senior producer Jim Vessella needing somewhere new to stick a bunch of Betacam tapes used to help make the Command & Conquer Remastered Collection. "One of the facilities people was like, 'I think there’s a room in the other building that has a couple of items like that'," Vessella recalled. "He took me over there, opened it up and it was like Indiana Jones… just, whoa. Knowing what I’d discovered with the Command & Conquer tapes, I really felt like we had to find out what was on all these tapes."

The producer was able to convince EA's bosses that this stuff was worth the work needed to stick it back on storefronts, and lo, an EA blog post gushing about their vault's door having slid aside to reveal this pile of retro games. The collection includes restored versions of the following games, all with "robust quality-of-life updates and higher resolution Full-Motion Videos (FMV) from the Master tapes":

Meanwhile, the rest of the lineup is filled out by "faithful ports" of the following:

On Steam, all of these are currently on sale for less than a fiver each, with the prices of the most expensive ones set to rise to just under £10 when the introductory offer expires on November 19th.

It's good news in preservation terms, though I'm not sure it totally washes away the bad vibes of EA's Saudi and private equity owners reportedly looking to take over Unity.

Steyning Town edged out by AFC Croydon Athletic in thriller

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The sides were separated by just two points in the table and the Barrowmen headed into the fixture full of confidence following an impressive 2-0 victory over Sittingbourne in their previous outing.

The opening ten minutes proved to be a fairly even contest, with neither side able to establish control.

Did you know with an ad-lite subscription to SussexWorld, you get 70% fewer ads while viewing the news that matters to you.

The first meaningful opportunity did not arrive until the 18th minute, when James Taylor tried his luck from outside the box. However, his effort was comfortably gathered by Steyning goalkeeper Alfie Mansell.

Steyning continued to struggle to create any real attacking threat. Croydon began to find more opportunities, with Jayden Raymond testing Mansell in the 24th minute. The Steyning keeper pushed the ball behind for a corner.

That brought further danger for the hosts, but Harvey Woolard produced an important goal-line clearance. Steyning looked to respond four minutes later from a corner, but Croydon keeper Amaadou Tangarn was on hand to punch the delivery clear.

Croydon continued to threaten, with Luke Dreher trying his luck in the 34th minute. Once again, Mansell was equal to the effort.

The hosts finally managed to register a shot on target in the 38th minute as Ryan Worrall struck a low effort towards goal, but his attempt drifted wide.

And Steyning’s resistance was broken just two minutes later. Raymond found himself unmarked and made no mistake, driving a low effort into the bottom corner to give Croydon the lead.

Things quickly went from bad to worse for Steyning as Raymond struck again in the 42nd minute. The forward was perfectly positioned to turn home a close-range tap-in, doubling Croydon’s advantage and leaving the hosts with an uphill battle.

Steyning attempted to find a response before the break, with Jesse Sentamu sending an effort over the crossbar in the 45th minute. Another opportunity followed from a corner but the resulting header also sailed over.

Steyning returned for the second half looking to turn the game around, but once again made a slow start, struggling to find a way through Croydon’s organised defensive line.

The hosts eventually found a breakthrough in the 61st minute. A deflected cross earned Steyning a corner, and following a scramble inside the area, substitute Max Blencowe managed to find the back of the net to make it 2-1.

Croydon continued to hold their defensive shape and limit Steyning’s opportunities. A free-kick for the hosts in the 66th minute was comfortably caught by goalkeeper Amaadou Tangarn, before James Taylor delivered a dangerous cross a minute later.

The Croydon defence could only spoon the ball behind for another corner, but Steyning were unable to make anything of the resulting set-piece.

However, the momentum was beginning to shift. Just two minutes later, substitute Alex Brewer produced a moment of real quality, striking a beautiful effort from outside the box to level the scores at 2-2 and complete an impressive comeback.

Mansell was called into action twice in quick succession, producing two important saves to keep Steyning level and prevent Croydon from immediately restoring their advantage.

But the visitors were not prepared to settle for a point. In the 77th minute, Josh Williams produced a brilliant finish to put Croydon back in front at 3-2.

Steyning pushed hard for another equaliser in the closing stages, but Croydon remained disciplined at the back, quickly closing down space and preventing the hosts from delivering the ball into dangerous areas.

A late opportunity arrived in the 88th minute, with Blencowe looking to grab his second, but his effort was comfortably dealt with by Tangarn. Brewer then came close to finding another late goal in the 90th minute, only to send his effort over the crossbar.

Croydon’s Noah Watson received a yellow card in the 94th minute for time-wasting as Steyning struggled to maintain possession during the closing moments. The hosts earned one final corner in the 95th minute after another effort went wide, but the set-piece came to nothing.

The final whistle confirmed a frustrating 3-2 defeat for Steyning, who had shown tremendous character to fight back from two goals down and restore parity, only for Williams’ late intervention to deny them a share of the points.

Steyning are 15th and host Horley Town on Saturday.

Protesters Break Into Microsoft Data Center Site And Hang ‘FCK AI’ Banner

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

As the global markets dive in reaction to the Democratic sweep in the U.S. midterm elections, fearful of a party willing to take a stand on the catastrophic spread of AI data centers, the question is being asked whether the public’s hatred of these environment-demolishing monstrosities could have played a part in how people voted. At the same time, protests against the development of town-sized data centers are springing up all around the world. In the latest example, as reported by ReutersÂ (via The Verge), Microsoft’s construction site in Amsterdam was broken into this morning by around 100 people, some of whom climbed a crane and hung a banner reading “FCK AI.”

Others, according toÂ The Verge, revealed a banner reading, “Microsoft powers genocide,” referencing the corporate behemoth’s ongoing support of the Netanyahu regime in Israel, along with “Free Palestine” and “Microsoft has got to go.”

Footage of the protesters has been uploaded to YouTube, showing people scaling the gates and piling into Microsoft’s construction site, with some scaling a crane to strap a makeshift banner reading “FCK AI” down its main arm, while others march and chant, the messaging seemingly split between those campaigning against data centers and those objecting to the Israeli attacks on Palestine.

Amsterdam has already taken a harder line than many other areas regarding the building of data centers, announcing last year that it would no longer approve projects submitted after the end of 2023, nor the expansion of previously approved sites, citing that the city lacks the space and the power to support them. The city said it wouldn’t start considering new pitches until 2030.

Meanwhile, polling suggests that the widespread construction of data centers in the U.S. was a factor in how people voted during the midterms. 56 percent of those surveyed by AP reported that the topic was a “crucial or major” factor in who they voted for, and 76 percent were against data centers being built within their own community.
