Plant These 7 Flower Seeds in November for Gorgeous Early Blooms Next Year

These 7 Flower Seeds to Sow in Fall Are the Best Investment You'll Make for Next Year's Garden

8 Fling-and-Forget Seeds to Scatter in Fall for Flowers that Grow Themselves – it’s Practically Magic!

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Peonies are pretty, we know, but peony-like tulips give you way more petals for far less effort, and you can plant the spring-flowering bulbs in November.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

We all adore the richly-colored, ruffled petals of peonies but if you’ve only got one plant in your garden, the show’s over in little more than a week. Plant some peony-like tulip bulbs, however, and you can enjoy months of very similar-looking flowers from early April all through May and sometimes even into June. These double tulips couldn’t be easier to grow, and this month is the exact right time to plant the bulbs.

Most tulips have six petals but a natural genetic hiccup capitalized upon by plant breeders means that some cultivars can have up to 50 packed into a single bloom. They’re known as double tulips, and their petal-explosion flowers look remarkably similar to peony blooms. Typically hardy in zones 3-8, they’re some of the best tulip varieties to kickstart spring in your garden, and the most striking spring-flowering bulbs to plant in November.

The trick to enjoying months of flowers is to pick a pair of tulip varieties, one that opens early and one that blooms a little later. Early-flowering double tulips (officially known as peony-like tulips) begin blooming early in April. These tend to have shorter, sturdy 12-16 inch-high stems so stand up well to spring storms. Their tight layers of petals protect the flower core from cold weather, too, so each bloom can last for around 3 weeks – far longer than a standard tulip.

Late-flowering double tulips (known as peony-flowered tulips) bloom late April through May and are even more spectacular with huge flower heads on taller 16-24 inch-high stems. Because they're so top-heavy, these are more vulnerable to toppling in strong winds so in an exposed garden, give them a sheltered spot.

So, if you love peony flowers and you're wondering what to plant in November, look no further. Part of the Weekend Wins series, planting double tulips that look like peonies is one of those easy garden jobs that brings a big reward.

When shopping for double tulips, it's important that bulbs are big enough to fuel all those petals so early in the year, so always buy from a quality supplier. All our recommendations are for 10 bulbs.

‘Lorenzo’ gets spring off to a cheery start with long-lasting blooms in a luminous apricot-orange softly edged in creamy yellow, all through April.

With enormous blooms opening in late April and peaking through May, ‘Gudoshnik Double' has painterly petals streaked in yellow, orange and red.

A few double tulips are fragranced, and 'Avant Garde’ opens its buds in early April to release a sweet, floral perfume with beautifully fresh undertones.

In late April, ‘Pistache’ brings more creamy-white petals, this time splashed with wine red, and a wonderful scent of almonds and pistachios into May.

‘Foxy Foxtrot’ wakes up early in April, opening a lovely lemon yellow then gaining rich, warm tones of apricot, salmon-pink and honeyed gold.

In mid-to-late April, ‘Amazing Grace’ starts in a soft lavender pink with green accents, then shade-shifts into a deep rose tone that lasts deep into May.

In early April, ‘Royal Acres’ blooms open into bowls of intensely-colored, velvety magenta petals, often with a silvery sheen along the ruffled edges.

‘Dream Touch’ takes over the show from late April through May with enormous purple blooms, their striking petals prettily edged in white.

Though officially classified as a late tulip, ‘Mariage’ blooms in mid-April with deeply ruffled flowers in the prettiest pale rose-pink, and lasts into May.

‘Wild Romance’ adds creamy white petals with blushing pink picotee edges from late April through May, opening to reveal dramatic jet-black stamens.

‘Marshmallow’ opens in early April with a splash of bi-colored raspberry pink and ivory white petals, with a few green streaks for good measure.

Keeping the drama going, ‘Black Hero’, blooms from late April through May with ruffles of velvety deep-maroon petals that have a super sheen.

Growing spectacular double tulip flowers from fall-planted bulbs couldn’t be easier, and November is the exact right time to get them in the ground.

Following BioWare's shock closure, a new documentary capturing its final moments is coming next year

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

A documentary detailing the history of beloved Mass Effect studio BioWare is releasing next year, and will cover last month's shock closure of the studio, following the private acquisition of owner EA.

Mass Effect: The BioWare Story (per Deadline) has been written and directed by Akash Sherman and produced for Canada's Super Channel. It documents the studio from the early days after its founding in 1995 to the release of barn-burner hits like Mass Effect and Dragon Age. Filming began two years prior to the announcement of BioWare's closure and continued throughout, with Deadline reporting the documentary will "capture BioWare’s final chapter."

The BioWare Story includes interviews with studio founders Ray Muzyka, Greg Zeschuk, and Augustine Yip, alongside Mass Effect director Casey Hudson, other BioWare developers, and Shepard voice actor Jennifer Hale. That's alongside former EA CEO John Riccitiello, LucasFilm Games vice president Douglas Reilly, and creator of The Game Awards Geoff Keighley.

"The BioWare founders are my hometown heroes,” writer and director Sherman said in a statement accompanying the documentary news. "It's a privilege and responsibility to share the journey of underdogs who came together to create deeply beloved storytelling. Their golden era kindled a spark in me and in an entire generation. With the studio's recent closure, the film is now a profound valediction."

For fans outside Canada, Mass Effect: The BioWare Story is planned for a major film festival run and a world premiere next year. No exact release date has been established as of writing.

Members of the games industry have lambasted EA's decision to close Bioware in the weeks since the news was shared, with legendary designer Warren Spector writing, "Anyone who participated in this travesty should be ashamed". Meanwhile, a petition has reached enough signatures for the Canadian government to potentially rescue the studio from closure. Whether or not it actually does… Well, weirder things have happened?

Trevor Lawrence is having by far the best season of his career

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Trevor Lawrence has so much talent for the quarterback position that if freshmen could declare for the NFL draft, Lawrence probably would have been the first overall pick in 2019, when he had just led Clemson to a national championship as a true freshman. Instead, Lawrence stayed three years at Clemson and was the first pick in 2021, viewed as a sure-thing quarterback prospect.

In Year 6, Lawrence finally looks like everything he was supposed to be.

Lawrence is having by far the best year of his NFL career, with career-high stats across the board that are constrained only by the fact that the Jaguars win so many blowouts that Lawrence doesn’t throw many passes in the fourth quarter.

So far this season, Lawrence has completed 146 of 224 passes for 1,932 yards, with 18 touchdowns, four interceptions and an NFL-leading passer rating of 111.7. Project Lawrence’s totals to a 17-game season, and he’s on pace to finish with 4,106 yards, 38 touchdowns and nine interceptions. Those are MVP numbers.

Lawrence’s rookie year was a disaster, as he led the league in interceptions while playing on a chaotic Jaguars team coached by Urban Meyer, whose only foray into NFL coaching lasted less than one season. Lawrence and the Jaguars got a lot better once they kicked Meyer to the curb, but even in the next three years with Doug Pederson, an offensive coach with a Super Bowl-winning pedigree, Lawrence didn’t live up to the pre-draft hype.

Playing in Liam Coen’s offense, something has changed. Last year the Jaguars went 13-4, winning double-digit games for the first time since drafting Lawrence, and this year Lawrence and the Jaguars are even better. Lawrence’s average of 8.6 yards per pass is the best of his career by almost a yard and a half, a remarkable improvement for any quarterback to make in his sixth season.

Lawrence has thrown touchdown passes on 8 percent of his attempts, the best in the NFL this season and by far the best mark of his career. And after getting sacked a career-high 41 times last year, Lawrence has gotten much more comfortable reading the pass rush in his second year in Coen’s offense and has only been sacked 13 times this season.

Lawrence’s numbers would look even better if the Jaguars weren’t so often taking their foot off the gas late in games because they’re blowing opponents out. Lawrence has 1,196 yards and 12 touchdowns in the first halves of games this season, but he only has 736 yards and six touchdowns in the second halves of games.

The Jaguars currently have the fourth-best odds to win the Super Bowl, and Lawrence has the fifth-best odds to win MVP. That might be underselling them, and him. The Jaguars may just be the best team, with the best quarterback, in the NFL right now.

Inside the indecision at Man United and the question that will decide Michael Carrick’s future

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

A vintage Old Trafford comeback, encouraging underlying numbers and doubts over the list of potential replacements mean there is a growing sense that Carrick could yet survive the international break, writes Miguel Delaney, even if they lose to Aston Villa on Sunday

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

As Manchester United returned to the training ground after this week’s Champions League heroics, some there couldn’t help but repeat an old football truth: “Everything feels completely different after a win.”

That will be even more pronounced now, given that United have just ended their longest winless run in 40 years. The 3-2 win over Roma was their first victory since the 4-0 over Sabah, 10 games ago.

Amid the raucous celebrations from a vintage comeback, there was of course considerable relief. It at least, for now, puts off a huge call.

United have been racked by indecision over the future of Michael Carrick. Numerous sources say there is a sense that no one was actually willing to make a call on the manager, a problem compounded by the fact they have “little idea” over a replacement.

In fairness, there are questions and debates over all of the main alternatives.

United are understood to be much less sold on Eddie Howe than perception would have it. Sebastian Hoeness is highly rated, but his appointment would still involve a huge Ruben Amorim-like leap from Stuttgart. Fabian Hurzeler might struggle without Brighton’s structure amid higher stakes, and there are doubts over whether United can even afford the compensation. Cesc Fabregas is impressing at Como but isn’t going anywhere for now, and is viewed as Mikel Arteta’s eventual successor at Arsenal.

Julian Nagelsmann’s stock has significantly dropped, meanwhile. While United’s Christoph Vivell is said to be a fan, others in the hierarchy will be aware that Bayern Munich would not speak highly about his time there. The players didn’t respond well to his approach or personality, and the image persists of Nagelsmann arriving to training on a skateboard. It is hard not to think of a line from The Sopranos. In the same way “a don doesn’t wear shorts”, a Manchester United manager doesn’t have a skateboard.

You can’t imagine that of Antonio Conte, a title winner at four different clubs who is one of the very few to have done it at the required level. The question is whether United’s squad is of the level to ensure they would at least get the good season you usually do out of the Italian, before an inevitable blow-up.

If all of this sounds harsh on Carrick given he’s just won again, it’s of course about the reality that all of the same concerns could rush back after the next game.

And that next game is against Aston Villa’s Unai Emery, who looks set to stay Aston Villa's Unai Emery. As has been said on these pages many times, football has that extraordinary capacity to throw up a narratively fitting fixture at a moment when a story is huge.

So, on the 40th anniversary of Sir Alex Ferguson’s appointment, United welcome a coach who some in the hierarchy do see as a potential figurehead for the modern age.

And, up until Tuesday, this last game before the international break had been cast as an inevitable curtain closer – at least for Carrick.

A burgeoning Villa side were surely just going to expose and exploit United’s myriad problems, just before a lot of time to think.

Except, United finally got that win, just as Emery’s team endure a bump ahead of a trip to a stadium where they have a dismal record. Villa have only won one game at Old Trafford in 15 years, and Emery has never won there at all.

If this all sounds an overt focus on psychology and form, the truth is it especially matters to United right now.

One of a few reasons they haven’t yet made a call on Carrick – to go with indecision – is because of the underlying numbers.

The data shows that United haven’t been quite as bad as they’ve appeared, even if it still hasn’t been great. The numbers have been almost identical to last season.

One belief is that the team, this time, have found themselves on the wrong side of the margins, which are very thin, in a complete reversal of last season. A few key moments have gone against them, which has further affected confidence at a fragile moment.

The whole performance against Chelsea summed this up. United were good in the first half, only to fail to take some big chances, and then buckled as Lisandro Martinez was sent off.

That has come amid a sense they didn’t back Carrick to the extent they should have in the summer, which is why they are just trying to get through this period and then do more business in January.

The hope has been that the team just needed that one win, that break, to end this current spiral.

It’s entirely possible that’s what the Roma victory may represent.

United themselves have enough experience of this.

They’ve experienced almost this exact situation before, too. Back in October 2021, just a month before Ole Gunnar Solskjaer was sacked, United came back from 2-0 down in the Champions League to beat a Gian Piero Gasperini Serie A team 3-2. It was then Atalanta rather than Roma, but it was still the same problem: a temporary balm.

And, just like then, it’s entirely possible the Roma win may lull United into a thinking that other clubs constantly say is so damaging to them. That is that they are always just “a few tweaks” from being there, when they still ultimately need that deep overhaul. Even if they beat Villa and go on a better run, does anyone really believe there won’t eventually be a regression?

The Ineos leadership have admittedly attempted to start that process, but it’s so far only been an attempt. They’re trying. They’re not yet succeeding.

As one chief executive who has direct experience of this process says, “it’s not actually difficult to figure out, but it’s hard to execute. What is absolutely crucial is to get quality people in the right positions.”

Again, a vision around which everything works and the nerve to commit to it. Do United have any of that?

Another issue right now is that the entire place is said to be enduring extreme emotional swings. The performances are of course the main factor, but so is the media industrial complex around the club. There's this constant noise.

Sir Jim Ratcliffe is so conscious of what Gary Neville’s United empire says, for example, that he did an interview with him.

The events of the last week show Villa can be given to emotional swings themselves, but the action over Emery’s future – and anticipated new contract – also shows a decisiveness that has been missing at United.

For United’s part, it is riskier for them, given their scale, to do what Villa did with Emery and give a manager the keys to the club.

That is also a model they’ve long wanted to move away from. They really don’t need to get into yet another cycle that just goes around and around.

For now, an uncertainty remains, right up to what next for the team.

World of Hyatt Card Limited Time Bonus Offer: Earn Four Free Night Awards

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

We’ve just seen a new limited time welcome offer rolled out on the World of Hyatt Credit Card, making it the ideal time to apply. I’d consider this to be one of the most lucrative hotel credit cards out there — it’s a product you don’t get just for the bonus, but instead, for the long term value that it offers.

Let’s take a look at the details of the card bonus and eligibility requirements, as well as the long term value.

Link: Learn more about the World of Hyatt Credit Card bonus offer

The World of Hyatt Credit Card is currently offering a limited time welcome bonus that consists of four Category 1-4 free night awards after spending $5,000 within the first three months.

World of Hyatt Category 4 properties retail for up to 25,000 points per night, so the absolute maximum value of this offer is 100,000 points. However, I of course wouldn’t value it that highly. That’s because these free night awards expire 12 months after they’re issued, and you also can’t top off these awards to redeem at more expensive properties. So actually maxing out the value of these isn’t easy.

One thing I should mention is that while I don’t love the lack of flexibility of this welcome offer, it’s significantly better than the standard bonus, which tends to be lackluster. Obviously Chase and Hyatt know that this is a card people will get for the long term value, rather than just for the bonus.

For what it’s worth, based on my latest miles & points valuations, I consider World of Hyatt points to be worth 1.4 cents each.

If you’re interested in applying for the World of Hyatt Credit Card, the eligibility rules for the bonus are pretty straightforward. This product is available to you if you don’t have this exact card, and haven’t received a new cardmember bonus on this card in the past 24 months.

So that means you could potentially earn the bonus if you’ve had the card in the past. Furthermore, eligibility is unrelated to whether you have the World of Hyatt Business Credit Card (learn more).

On top of that, Chase’s general application rules apply, including that you can typically be approved for at most two Chase cards every 30 days, and the Chase 5/24 rule is also worth considering (you may not be approved if you’ve opened five or more new card accounts in the past 24 months).

There’s not a consistent credit score you need to be approved for this card, though I’d hope to have a score in the “good” to “excellent” range, ideally 700+ points. However, people with lower scores have been approved, and people with higher scores have been rejected.

This is a card I’ve had for years, and one that I spend at least $15,000 on annually, so that I earn the second free night award, plus six additional elite nights. If you stay at Hyatts with any frequency, having this card should be a no-brainer. For more details, read my full World of Hyatt Card review.

If you’re looking for a Hyatt co-branded credit card but want a business card instead, then the World of Hyatt Business Credit Card is for you. The card is currently offering a bonus of 70,000 points after spending $7,000 within the first three months.

At my valuation of 1.4 cents per point, that’s roughly $980 in value, which more than offsets the $199 annual fee in year one.

Eligibility is straightforward, as you can’t currently hold this specific card, and you can’t have received a welcome bonus on it within the past 24 months. Having the personal World of Hyatt Credit Card doesn’t affect your eligibility, as you can hold both cards and earn bonuses on each. Standard Chase application rules apply.

For more details, read my World of Hyatt Business Card review, as well as my World of Hyatt personal vs. business card comparison.

The World of Hyatt Credit Card is currently offering a limited time welcome offer that can earn you four Category 1-4 free night awards upon completing minimum spending. Category 4 properties go for up to 25,000 points per night, so this is potentially quite a substantial welcome bonus.

However, the bonus isn’t the main reason you get this card. Instead, it’s about the long term value proposition, including elite nights toward status, an anniversary free night award, etc. Furthermore, it’s a card that’s worth spending $15,000 on per year, if you can swing it, so you can earn a second free night award and six more elite nights toward status.

What furious Spalletti shouted at Cabal during nervy Juventus end to 2-1 AZ victory

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Juventus made it back-to-back victories when they beat AZ Alkmaar 2-1 away from home in the Europa League on Thursday evening, but head coach Luciano Spalletti was still furious at his defenders for the way they handled the closing stages, telling Juan Cabal that he could punch him.

The Bianconeri took the lead through Teun Koopmeiners and saw their advantage doubled before the interval by Randal Kolo Muani, who has earned widespread praise for his performance in the Italian press.

Despite looking comfortable and without being particularly threatened by AZ Alkmaar for the majority of proceedings, Juventus did make life more difficult for themselves in the closing stages and ended up conceding in the 88th minute to Mexx Meerdink. It was then a nervous few minutes before the final whistle was sounded.

Spalletti wasn’t best pleased with what he saw in the final few minutes of the 2-1 victory over AZ Alkmaar, and a clip of his shouting at full-back Juan Cabal on the sideline has since done the rounds on social media.

As per La Gazzetta dello Sport, the furious Spalletti shouted ‘I’ll punch you’ while squaring up face-to-face with the Colombian.

Fortunately for the Bianconeri, they were able to get the result over the line in the end and Spalletti had calmed down by the time he spoke to the media after full-time.

“I am very satisfied, because we played well against a very strong team,” Spalletti told Sky Sport Italia after full-time.

“I remember when all of you laughed in my face when we beat NEC 5-0, you acted as if they were a ridiculous team, but NEC now have seven points in the Europa League.

“So perhaps people need to re-evaluate how they consider us, humility, and giving credit to a team without just constantly wanting to put pressure on.

“When we win a game, people act as if it was because the opponents were weak, or finding some excuses. We played a strong game against a great team tonight, and as we saw the moment our intensity dropped, they will take advantage.

“We’ve seen in the Champions League, Europa League, these sides might have a predictable way of doing things, but it becomes a strength when they can do it that many times. It is constantly hammering away at those situations, we were pinned back, worried about whether or not to push out from defence, and a through ball caused a goal.

“There were mad scrambles, if the game lasted for another three or four minutes, I don’t think we would’ve taken the result home.”

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Popular streamer IShowSpeed’s first Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time death came at the wings and beaks of a swarm of angry chickens.

Following the November 5 release of Nintendo’s highly anticipated Ocarina of Time remake, IShowSpeed has been busy playing through the critically acclaimed Switch 2 game.

He’s even gone as far as to dress up as the legendary Hero of Time himself, and seems to be having a great time trekking through the lands of Hyrule.

However, despite donning Link’s signature green tunic, his first death was far from heroic, as he fell for one of the oldest rules in the Zelda playbook: Don’t mess with the chickens.

The most deadly enemy in Ocarina of Time and the entire Zelda universe isn’t Ganondorf or his beast form, Ganon. No, every fan knows it’s the game’s Cuccos. These cute clucking chickens may look unassuming, but if you hit them, they’ll call upon their poultry pals and rain down a feathery hailstorm of pecks that will deplete your health bar in seconds.

It’s a rite of passage that every Zelda fan goes through, and it was no different for Speed when he decided to see if he could hit Cucco in Kakariko Village. “Alright, chat, let me see if I can hit this thing. Chat, you can’t hit the chicken!” the streamer shouted as the bird effortlessly dodged his sword swipes.

However, Speed was determined to stick the bird with the pointy end of his sword and kept pursuing his clucking target. After unleashing a barrage of slashes, the Cucoo finally decided it had had enough, and called out for its allies to stop Speed dead in his tracks.

AI Tokens Face Huge Sell-off as Global Markets Crash on AI Bubble Fears – What’s Next?

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

A passionate cryptocurrency and blockchain author qualified to cover every event in the crypto space. Researching minute occurrences and bringing new insights lie within the prime focus of my task.

A passionate cryptocurrency and blockchain author qualified to cover every event in the crypto space. Researching minute occurrences and bringing new insights lie within the prime focus of my task.

A passionate cryptocurrency and blockchain author qualified to cover every event in the crypto space. Researching minute occurrences and bringing new insights lie within the prime focus of my task.

AI bubble fears are rattling global markets as investors question infrastructure spending, valuations and the profitability of massive technology investments.

AI tokens face mounting selling pressure as weakening investor confidence and broader market uncertainty weigh on cryptocurrency sentiment.

Global financial markets are facing renewed turbulence as fears surrounding an AI bubble intensify, triggering a sharp sell-off across technology stocks and semiconductor companies. The correction, which began on Wall Street, has spread across Asia, with major indices in Japan, South Korea and Taiwan recording substantial losses as investors reassess the sustainability of massive AI investments and their potential returns.

The growing uncertainty has also spilt into the crypto market, where Bitcoin and several altcoins are facing renewed selling pressure. AI-based tokens like NEAR, TAO, RENDER, FET, etc., and a few more are struggling, raising questions about whether weakening confidence in the broader AI investment narrative is beginning to weigh on digital assets.

The growing concerns surrounding AI investments have extended beyond Wall Street, triggering a sharp correction across global equity markets. Technology and semiconductor stocks have faced significant selling pressure as investors reassess AI valuations. The losses were concentrated in semiconductor companies, which have benefitted significantly from rising demand for AI processors and data-centre infrastructure.

Key US market movements included the Nasdaq Composite with a drop of 5.5%, the S&P 500 with 4.4% and the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index with a 12.2% loss. Selling pressure then spread across Asia, where markets with significant exposure to the semiconductor & AI hardware industries posted substantial losses. South Korea’s KOSPI plunged 8.8% intraday, triggering a temporary halt, while Japan’s Nikkei and Taiwan’s TAIEX also recorded losses of 6% and 4.5%, respectively.

The latest correction highlights how closely global technology markets are tied to the AI investment cycle.

Bitcoin and major altcoins faced extensive selling pressure that erased the recent gains. The BTC price quickly fell below $76,000 after regaining $80,000, while the ETH price re-entered the consolidation zone around $2,225. The broader crypto market also experienced a significant correction, with losses extending beyond Bitcoin & Ethereum. The sell-off extended to AI-based cryptos that declined by 4.79% in the past 24 hours, with 86 of 106 tokens trading in negative territory.

The weakness highlights how the broader reassessment of AI-related investments is coinciding with declining confidence across digital assets.

The global sell-off has raised concerns about whether weakening confidence in AI investments could extend beyond technology stocks and cryptocurrencies. With semiconductor companies, major equity indices and AI-related tokens facing selling pressure, investors are becoming increasingly cautious about risk-sensitive assets.

The next few trading sessions will be crucial in determining whether this is a temporary correction or a prolonged market downturn. Continued weakness in AI investments, semiconductor demand and institutional risk appetite could keep pressure on global equities and cryptocurrencies. The bigger question is whether fears surrounding the AI bubble will remain confined to technology markets or trigger a wider risk-off environment.

Solana’s Shanghai event halted over unauthorized promotion: Report

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Solana’s promotional campaigns through domestic entities allegedly violated China’s crypto-related restrictions, an unidentified PBOC official told China’s Financial News.

Chinese authorities halted the Solana Accelerate event in Shanghai over alleged unauthorized promotional activities involving domestic entities, China’s state-backed Financial News reported on Friday.

The outlet cited an unidentified official from a department of the People’s Bank of China (PBOC), who said Solana had promoted its brand through domestic entities, including by placing advertisements on a building in Shanghai’s Lujiazui financial district.

The official also claimed that Solana provides technical services supporting the issuance and trading of cryptocurrencies, which he described as illegal financial activities under Chinese regulations.

It marked the second reported disruption of a Solana event in mainland China in the past two years, highlighting how the country’s restrictions extend beyond digital asset trading.  An October 2025 Solana event in Shenzhen was also cut short over venue capacity and public-safety concerns, according to an X announcement by Solana’s Chinese-speaking community.

In an Oct. 16 X post, Solana Foundation president Lily Liu said organizers postponed the Shanghai event to “comply with local requirements” after an event registration unexpectedly quadrupled, exceeding the event cap pre-registered with local authorities.

In a February notice, the PBOC and several other agencies doubled down on crypto-related restrictions and included a new ban on the unauthorized issuance of yuan-pegged stablecoins.

Apple confirms $7.5 billion bond sale following strong investor demand

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Following yesterday’s report that Apple was preparing to raise approximately $7 billion through a bond offering, the company has now finalized terms with the SEC, confirming a slightly larger amount. Here are the details.

Yesterday morning, Apple filed a preliminary prospectus with the SEC, outlining plans to issue a series of bonds maturing between 2028 and 2036.

Soon after, Bloomberg reported that the bond sale was targeting roughly $7 billion, with investor interest already surpassing $30 billion.

Now, Apple has filed the final pricing term sheet for the bond sale with the SEC, confirming a $7.5 billion offering across the seven previously announced series, with the following breakdown:

Floating-rate bonds, as the name implies, have interest rates that fluctuate over time based on certain market conditions, rather than remaining fixed.

Of that $7.5 billion, Apple expects to receive approximately $7.47 billion after deducting underwriting discounts and other offering expenses.

In essence, Apple will pay investors interest over the life of the bonds, then repay the full amount borrowed when each series reaches maturity. Apple may also choose to repay some or all of its fixed-rate bonds early.

As described in yesterday’s prospectus, the company intends to use the proceeds for “general corporate purposes,” which may include stock buybacks, dividends, acquisitions, and debt repayment.

Canadian Photographers Get Huge Tax Break In Proposed Federal Budget

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Canadian Photographers Get Huge Tax Break In Proposed Federal Budget

The Canadian Liberal government’s new Budget 2026 will carve out protections and support for Canadian artists, including photographers.

As Prime Minister Mark Carney wrote on X, formerly known as Twitter, “In Budget 2026, we’re backing the Canadian artists who tell our stories.

“That means if you’re a Canadian artist, you won’t pay tax on up to $45,000 of income derived from your creative work.”

In Budget 2026, we’re backing the Canadian artists who tell our stories.

That means if you’re a Canadian artist, you won’t pay tax on up to $45,000 of income derived from your creative work.

— Mark Carney (@MarkJCarney) November 5, 2026

The Professional Photographers of Canada (PPOC) shared the news on Facebook.

“Did you see the budget announcement that Canadian Artists won’t pay tax on up to $45,000 of income derived from their creative work?” PPOC writes.

“We’ll be monitoring the details closely as they unfold in order to help you understand what this means for Canadian photographers.”

In broad strokes, the proposed Canadian Budget 2026 includes a “Canada Creative Credit.” This new tax incentive is designed to “foster creativity, artistic production, and cultural entrepreneurship in Canada.”

“Artists, creators, musicians, and writers produce the stories, songs, and connections that stitch our country together,” Budget 2026 explains. “From Céline Dion to Jacob Tierney, Margaret Atwood to Edward Burtynsky, Canada continually raises the bar with its artistic output, impact, and creativity on the global stage.”

It’s great to see Canadian photographer Edward Burtynsky get a specific shoutout. He is among the most accomplished and acclaimed photographers working today.

However, before someone can become a Céline Dion or Edward Burtynsky, they must be able to get their feet under themselves and improve their craft. This is the focus of Canada’s proposed Creative Credit:

But before Canadian artists reach audiences around the world, they have to be able to build a life around their art here in Canada. For emerging artists, that means having the opportunity to experiment with ideas, hone their craft, and create new work. A creator’s work begins long before a book reaches a shelf, a song finds an audience, or a curtain rises. Supporting the next generation means valuing the creative process and creating the conditions for Canadian artists to develop their craft and contribute to Canada’s cultural life.

The Canadian government plans to support emerging artists through the new tax relief that Prime Minister Carney mentioned on social media. The government expects the credit to take the form of a non-refundable personal income tax credit that would, alongside existing tax relief for certain amounts of income, provide tax recognition for up to $45,000 of annual qualifying net business income derived from original and creative artistic works. Further details will be shared in the Spring Economic Update 2027.

The Canadian government believes that by strengthening and incentivizing meaningful professional artistic creation, it will ultimately strengthen its overall economy and culture.

“Canadian talent should have the opportunity to thrive,” the government explains.

Canada’s Budget 2026 has been introduced in the House of Commons and must still be passed to become law.

Budapest exhibition explores Van Gogh and Gauguin's artistic excursion to Montpellier

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The portico of Budapest’s Museum of Fine Art

Adventures with Van Gogh is a weekly blog by Martin Bailey, The Art Newspaper's long-standing correspondent and expert on the Dutch painter. Published on Fridays, stories range from newsy items about this most intriguing artist, to scholarly pieces based on meticulous investigations and discoveries.

Explore all of Martin’s adventures with Van Gogh here.

After Paul Gauguin arrived in Arles in October 1888 to stay with his friend Van Gogh, the two artists would go out to paint together and then socialise and discuss art in the evenings over a glass of wine. Their very different personalities, however, soon led to tensions. Living together in the confines of the Yellow House and sharing a small room as a studio, they began to row. As winter approached, and the weather made it impossible to work outside, the situation only worsened.

In mid-December, in an attempt to clear the air, Gauguin suggested that they should make a trip to Montpellier, 70 km west of Arles. Their goal was to visit the Musée Fabre, renowned for its fine collection of paintings.

The artistic result of this trip forms a central part of a major exhibition on Gauguin which opened this week at Budapest’s Museum of Fine Arts. Bonjour Monsieur Gauguin: The Circles of Post-Impressionists and Symbolists (until 28 February 2027) provides a wide-ranging exploration of the art of Gauguin and his associates. The show’s title comes from a key painting by the artist inspired by the Montpellier visit, Bonjour Monsieur Gauguin (late 1889).

Paul Gauguin’s Bonjour Monsieur Gauguin (late 1889)

Hammer Museum, Los Angeles (photograph The Art Newspaper)

Van Gogh and Gauguin travelled from Arles to Montpellier by train, which took around four hours. Although it has long been assumed that they made a day trip on either 16 or 17 December 1888, it is arguably more likely that they spent the night there and stayed for both days. The Musée Fabre had over 800 paintings on display, so they would surely have wanted time to savour the works. Many of the finest pictures were French, given by Alfred Bruyas, a local collector and patron of Gustave Courbet.

Gustave Courbet’s Bonjour Monsieur Courbet (1854)

For Gauguin, the highlight of the museum was Courbet’s Bonjour Monsieur Courbet (1854). This landscape composition includes the figure of Courbet himself, laden with his equipment and holding a walking pole. He is greeting Bruyas, who is accompanied by a bowing servant.

Gustave Courbet’s Bonjour Monsieur Courbet and Gauguin’s Bonjour Monsieur Gauguin in the Budapest exhibition

Gauguin, who was impressed with the Courbet, painted a work in response, a year later, in the Brittany fishing village of Le Pouldu. The main figure in this work is Gauguin himself, facing a Breton woman on the other side of a gate.

Superficially, the Gauguin painting bears little resemblance to the Courbet. Stylistically, Courbet’s smoothly painted picture contrasts with Gauguin’s rough brushwork. The compositions appear to have little in common, other than showing an artist encountering other people—and being greeted with a “good day” (and, incidentally, both scenes also include a dog).

The moods seem very different. Courbet appears thrilled to greet his patron and friend. Gauguin’s encounter with the Breton woman, however, is fleeting, as they happen to pass each other at a gate. The Budapest exhibition’s lead curator, Judit Geskó, sees the Gauguin picture as representing “the artist as a solitary wanderer”.

But despite the differences, Gauguin’s decision to entitle his work as he did shows that he was directly acknowledging the source of his inspiration, the Courbet. Gauguin’s title is actually inscribed on the painting, in the lower-left corner.

Gauguin made two versions of his composition: the one on loan to Budapest from the Hammer Museum in Los Angeles and the other (which was unavailable for borrowing) at the National Gallery in Prague. The Hammer picture, which was the original, was created to display on the dining room door in the small inn where he was staying in Le Pouldu.

As for Van Gogh, the Dutchman was particularly struck in the Musée Fabre by the portraits by Courbet. These included a self-portrait, The Man with the Pipe (around 1849), which may well have inspired Van Gogh one month later to paint his Self-Portrait with Bandaged Ear and Pipe (January 1889).

Bruyas was depicted by a range of artists and these portraits were also much admired by Van Gogh in the Montpellier museum. Vincent described Bruyas’ appearance to his brother Theo as “a gentleman with a beard, red hair, who looks damnably like you or me”.

Postcard of Musée Fabre, Montpellier (1905)

Both Gauguin and Van Gogh were great admirers of Courbet, who was very well represented in the Musée Fabre. Although they agreed about Courbet, their tastes for other artists were often rather different. Vincent wrote to Theo that their artistic discussions in Montpellier had been “excessively electric”. He explained: “We sometimes emerge from it with tired minds, like an electric battery after it’s run down.”

Despite these animated discussions, the inspirational visit seemed a success. Vincent reported back to Theo the day after their return that in the museum they had been “right in the midst of magic”. He added: “Gauguin said to me this morning, when I asked him how he felt: ‘that he could feel his old self coming back’, which gave me great pleasure.”

Sadly, the reconciliation did not last long: just four days later Van Gogh severely mutilated his ear. Although the trigger was probably news he had just received that Theo had become engaged (which Vincent feared would threaten his brother’s support), tensions with Gauguin were also a contributing factor.

Budapest’s Gauguin exhibition is ambitious, with 80 works by the French master and 70 by other artists. Although financial valuations for exhibitions are rarely revealed, The Art Newspaper can report that its total insurance value is €1.5bn.

Van Gogh’s Field with Irises near Arles (May 1888)

Van Gogh Museum, Amsterdam (Vincent van Gogh Foundation)

The show includes three works by Van Gogh: the landscape painting Field with Irises near Arles (May 1888, Van Gogh Museum, Amsterdam) and two rarely-displayed drawings from the collection of the Budapest museum, The Parsonage Garden at Nuenen in Winter (March 1884) and Haystacks (July 1888).

Van Gogh’s The Parsonage Garden at Nuenen in Winter (March 1884) and Haystacks (July 1888)

The Budapest exhibition has two Gauguin paintings dating from his stay with Van Gogh in Arles: the landscape Les Alyscamps (October 1888, Musée d’Orsay, Paris) and the portrait Madame Roulin (November-December 1888, Saint Louis Art Museum). Both of these subjects were also tackled by Van Gogh.

Gauguin’s Les Alyscamps (October 1888) and Madame Roulin (November-December 1888)

Musée d’Orsay, Paris and Saint Louis Art Museum

Among the other Gauguin highlights in the show is Sunflowers on an Armchair (1901, Bührle Collection, on loan to the Kunsthaus Zurich). This is one of four still-life paintings inspired by Van Gogh’s favourite motif—sunflowers. Gauguin painted these in the Marquesas Islands (north of Tahiti), with flowers grown from seeds he was sent by a friend from Paris.

Gauguin’s Sunflowers on an Armchair (1901)

Bührle Collection, on loan to the Kunsthaus Zurich

The museum estimates that the Gauguin exhibition could see up to 350,000 visitors, a huge number for a medium-sized European capital. However, even this won’t match Geskó's Van Gogh show in 2006, which attracted 486,000 people to the museum to see the Dutch painter's works.

Martin Bailey is a leading Van Gogh specialist and special correspondent for The Art Newspaper. He has curated exhibitions at the Barbican Art Gallery, Compton Verney/National Gallery of Scotland and Tate Britain.

Martin has written a number of bestselling books on Van Gogh’s years in France: The Sunflowers Are Mine: The Story of Van Gogh's Masterpiece (Frances Lincoln 2013, UK and US), Studio of the South: Van Gogh in Provence (Frances Lincoln 2016, UK and US), Starry Night: Van Gogh at the Asylum (White Lion Publishing 2018, UK and US) and Van Gogh’s Finale: Auvers and the Artist’s Rise to Fame (Frances Lincoln 2021, UK and US). The Sunflowers are Mine (2024, UK and US) and Van Gogh’s Finale (2024, UK and US) are also now available in a more compact paperback format.

His other recent books include Living with Vincent van Gogh: The Homes & Landscapes that shaped the Artist (White Lion Publishing 2019, UK and US), which provides an overview of the artist’s life. The Illustrated Provence Letters of Van Gogh has been reissued (Batsford 2021, UK and US). My Friend Van Gogh/Emile Bernard provides the first English translation of Bernard’s writings on Van Gogh (David Zwirner Books 2023, UKand US).

Thinking Machines’ new model takes the lead in western open-weight AI

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Another competitive model developed outside of China could change the open-weight landscape.

Thinking Machines Lab, a small AI startup from ex-OpenAI CTO Mira Murati, has released its latest model, Inkling 2 Small — and according to third-party tester Artificial Analysis, it’s set to give Chinese models a run for their lead. It’s technically proprietary until now, but Thinking Machines said it will release its weights next week.

Also: Open weights vs. closed: An AI civil war’s afoot

Chinese companies have led the pack on open models over the last few years. Models from DeepSeek and Moonshot regularly top leaderboards and rival the capabilities of much more expensive, closed models from OpenAI and Anthropic.

Since DeepSeek’s R1 performed similarly to OpenAI’s o1 in January 2025, Chinese open models have given investors and high-paying enterprise customers reasons to rethink their expensive contracts with US-based labs. Western labs creating more competitive open models changes the landscape.

Thinking Machines’ latest model has 594 billion parameters and supports image inputs but outputs only text. The company noted its web and game design capabilities and that it evaluated the model’s approach to coding problems alongside the code it generated during testing.

“The result is a model that is both stronger at coding and easier to use inside real agent loops,” the company said.

Also: Nvidia takes another step forward for US open-weight AI

Inkling 2 Small scored a 31 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, a composite test “for tracking progress toward artificial general intelligence” that combines benchmark scores spanning coding, science, and general categories. A score of 31 is above average for models in its class and is tied with Gemini 3.8 Flash. For context, Gemini 4 Carbon and Claude Opus 5.5 top the index at 60 and 59, respectively.

Thinking Machines said it will release the model’s weights next week, a practice that is becoming customary. Given the heightened security concerns around AI in general, AI companies spend a few weeks testing the to-be-open model with cybersecurity teams and safety reviewers before releasing the weights, rather than leading with the weights out of the gate.

French AI lab Mistral recently released its Le Chonk model via the same staggered process — it now keeps pace with OpenAI’s GPT-6.1 Terra in coding abilities, which is rare for western-made open models.

Also: Mistral’s Le Chonk is now a top open model for coding

“[Thinking Machines’] plan to release its weights would make it the highest-scoring open weights model developed outside China based on current rankings,” Artificial Analysis said.

“Inkling 2 Small is amongst the leading models in intelligence and reasonably priced when comparing to other models of similar price. It’s also notably slow per task and very verbose,” Artificial Analysis said. The organization also called Inkling 2 Small’s pricing “somewhat expensive” at $0.30 per one million input tokens, since the median cost for its class is $0.24.

Still, the model’s progress over its predecessor stands out to Artificial Analysis reviewers: Inkling 2 Small jumped 41% in automation capabilities and 20% in coding abilities. However, most models excel in certain areas or on specific benchmarks, making it hard to deem one or another the outright best coding model overall.

Also: I tried replacing Claude Code with a local AI stack that’s completely free

Inkling 2 Small also made some gains in accuracy, Artificial Analysis said.

“On AA-Omniscience v1.1, the hallucination rate falls from Inkling Small’s 61% to 27%, while accuracy rises from 32% to 34% and the attempt rate falls from 77% to 53%,” its review reported.

Ratchet & Clank: Ranger Rumble Shutting Down In 2027

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Ratchet & Clank: Ranger Rumble, a new mobile hero shooter spin-off, launched globally in October, but it's already being shut down.

"We've made the difficult decision to cease development," developer Oh BiBi announced. "The final planned update will be rolling out November 6th, 2026, and the app will be removed from stores on November 21st, 2026. Players who already have the game will be able to continue playing until May 1st, 2027, when the game’s servers will permanently shut down."

Oh BiBi explained that once the servers have been taken offline, "the game will no longer load" and all player data will be wiped from the servers. In the meantime, in-app purchases have been disabled, and the Super Pass is now free.

Ranger Rumble is the first new entry in the Ratchet & Clank series since Rift Apart in 2021, set in Captain Qwark's "totally safe simulation." Each match is essentially a training mission, with 12 unique heroes spanning from Zed to Tempest and Lump, with new Lombax characters like Sprocket, Chip, Widget, and Mopz thrown into the mix. Bizarrely, however, Ratchet was an event exclusive character.

At this point, us Ratchet fans will take what we can get.

The game also featured a few different modes to choose from, including a battle royale and Rocket League-esque match up:

You might be surprised to hear that there was a free-to-play Ratchet & Clank hero shooter on mobile, but it was pretty well-received among those who did play it. The game currently holds a 4.5 star rating on Google Play, but with just 100,00+ downloads, only a fraction of fans ever got around to trying it.

The original Ratchet & Clank is finally joining the PS Plus classics catalogue later this year.

How to get Sweet Dreams Sprite in Fortnite Treasure Hunters

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Treasure Hunters mini-season in Fortnite comes with its own array of unique Sprites you can find and collect around the island. This season is shorter than usual, so you’ll have to act fast to find them all. Perhaps the trickiest one to get is the Sweet Dreams Sprite.

Sprite hunting is generally a pretty tough process, especially for Mythic variants like this one. If you’re having trouble tracking it down, here’s how to get the Sweet Dreams Sprite in Fortnite.

The Sweet Dreams Sprite is a random loot drop item, which means you need to go looting around the island and hope you find it. For the most part, how easy or difficult it is to collect comes down to luck and random chance, although you can focus heavily on Sprite hunting to increase your odds.

Sprites can sometimes be found wandering around the island, but rare ones like this one are very uncommon to spot this way. Instead, you’ll want to focus on using Sprite Wanted Posters to dig up and open Sprite Chests and opening Vaults to find Sprite Chests. These two methods are the overall best ways to add Sprites to your collection.

Read what’s behind the latest news in gaming (with a bit of snark) by subscribing to the Destructoid Checkpoint newsletter!

If you’re determined to add the Sweet Dreams Sprite to your collection, there is one guaranteed method you can use to get it. This method takes a lot of patience, hard work, and dedication, but it does ensure you end up with this elusive Sprite in your collection.

Here’s a complete breakdown of how to guarantee you get the Sweet Dreams Sprite.

There is no guaranteed spawn location for the Sweet Dreams Sprite in Fortnite. Instead, you must loot around and hope you encounter it randomly, or work hard to use the method mentioned above to ensure you find one.

The Sweet Dreams Sprite grants you a random item each time it reaches a new level. Once it’s fully maxed out at level five, it grants you an array of Mythic loot. The value of the loot it provides increases with each level.

Duo Completes Baffin Island Circuit By Skiing, Hiking, and Packrafting

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Caleb McDaniels, 25, and Henry Penfold, 22, have completed their impressive expedition around Baffin Island. The Americans combined skiing, hiking, and packrafting to almost circle the world’s fifth-largest island. McDaniels told ExplorersWeb they did about 20% skiing and the rest was evenly split between backpacking and packrafting.

The pair began and ended in Iqaluit, Nunavut’s capital. The final section of their trip was also the longest, a 1,130km stretch with no villages after Igloolik to resupply. They made light work of it, knocking it out in 40 days and bringing their total distance to 3,262km over 145 days, from their late May start to their return to Iqaluit in mid-October. By then, the oncoming Arctic winter gave them few hours of light to work with.

The pair’s completed route around Baffin Island. Photo: Caleb McDaniels

With no resupply points, the pair left Igloolik with 82kg of food. They hoped to do it in seven weeks but brought even more food, because they weren’t sure how long that giant giant section would actually take, explained Caleb McDaniels.

“With no way to carry that much weight in our packs, we loaded up our packrafts and paddled the first 400 miles [644km]. Then, for the final 300 miles [483km], we heaved on our packs to walk overland,” they explained.

The paddling portion went quickly, aided by some ingenuity. “Much of the time, we were able to sail using our trekking poles as masts and panels cut out of an old tent for sails. Speeding along at over five mph, we did some incredible downwinding, spray flying from our bows.”

Using makeshift sails. Photo: Caleb McDaniels

They also effectively halved the 480km trek by packrafting over two large lakes, including Nettilling, the world’s largest lake on an island. Yet there was no way to ease the final 160km slog into Iqaluit.

Postholing through early winter snow back to Iqaluit. Photo: Caleb McDaniels

“It was more of a postholing session — we slowly picked our way across snow-covered boulder fields and partly frozen marshlands. Constantly breaking through thin ice over the tundra marshes, we walked in our drysuits to keep our legs and feet dry. Each morning required half an hour of chipping ice and melting out our shoes, which invariably froze solid,” they said.

McDaniels and Penfold had planned a roughly circular route around the island, but “we weren’t trying to circumnavigate Baffin,” McDaniels told ExplorersWeb. Two previous parties had circumnavigated the island by dogteam, but McDaniels and Penfold’s route was driven by aesthetic considerations rather than to satisfy a catchy trip definition.

Slogging overland. Photo: Caleb McDaniels

They chose their route after looking at satellite images and enjoyed both spectacular scenery and some special wildlife sightings, including at least one that was a little too close for comfort.

“One morning, as I went to fill our water bottles, I stumbled upon a sleeping polar bear hidden behind a low rise. Only a few yards away, it lifted its massive white head and glared at me for a moment before returning to its nap in the sun,” McDaniels wrote. “I carefully retreated behind the rise and yelled over to Henry to get the shotgun and flares out just in case. We quickly packed our boats and got on the water.”

In Foxe Basin, they almost collided with a sleeping walrus, and on Nettilling Lake, they met one of the resident seals. “He was immensely curious and not at all afraid. He swam around our boats for half an hour, playing with our stern lines and swimming back and forth between us. At one point, I put my hand into the water, and he brushed it with his nose!”

Martin Walsh is a writer and editor for ExplorersWeb.

Martin spent most of the last 20 years backpacking the world on a shoestring budget. Whether it was hitchhiking through Syria, getting strangled in Kyrgyzstan, touring Cambodia’s medical facilities with an exceedingly painful giant venomous centipede bite, chewing khat in Ethiopia, or narrowly avoiding various toilet-related accidents in rural China, so far, Martin has just about survived his decision-making.

Based in Da Lat, Vietnam, Martin can be found in the jungle trying to avoid leeches while chasing monkeys.

ExplorersWeb is part of AllGear Digital's portfolio of media brands.

We've been early adopters of Rust since 2018 and a lot of our infra, voice tech, and even some of the current client is already Rust, the holy grail was getting the whole client there. That means Electron goes away, and so does React Native, mobile keeps native UI but shares app… https://t.co/2CgWyOzaq0 pic.twitter.com/IdOGd4nUglNovember 4, 2026

Open up Task Manager and check how much memory Discord is currently hogging on your PC. If it's anything like mine, it's probably north of 500 MB. Alas, things should change soon, as Discord is moving the client completely over to a new programming language. It might not sound like that big of a deal, but Discord's CTO, Stanislav Vishnevskiy, shows it running on as little as 290 MB of memory.

Discord is switching from Electron to Rust. Electron is a software framework, a building block that allows JavaScript apps (among others) to run standalone on desktops. Built on Chromium, the underlying model behind Google Chrome, there is an overhead here that swapping to Rust aims to fix.

Vishnevskiy says, "We've been early adopters of Rust since 2018 and a lot of our infra, voice tech, and even some of the current client is already Rust, the holy grail was getting the whole client there. That means Electron goes away, and so does React Native, mobile keeps native UI but shares app code with desktop via Rust."

Though some suspected Discord would be swapping to Rust using Tauri (an open-source framework for desktop and mobile platforms), Vishnevskiy says it's actually using GPUI (a framework written in Rust), but "We have our own patches on top of it".

Vishnevskiy also says the goal is to get the program into testing with users soon, but no official date has been given. Either way, with Vishnevskiy showing off a resource demand of 290.2 MB whilst active, that's still a pretty big dip below what it runs at now. Mine is currently flitting between 500 and 600 MB, and my colleague Jacob saw it go up to 785 MB running on Windows.

The new Discord client using less RAM is just part of the story. Clicking around feels instant, I can't go back. Check out the boot time. https://t.co/YF9g9K30b9 pic.twitter.com/x7ayyWBVQJNovember 5, 2026

Reportedly, the new Discord client is also faster to use, booting much quicker. Vishnevskiy showed two laptops, one with the old client and one with the new, and the new client opens up significantly faster (six seconds versus one in my approximation).

Naturally, we are hearing this from the mouth of the company, so we'll have to get the new Discord client in our hands to see how much faster and how much more efficient it really is, but anyway, to get a little more out of your current memory will always be a boon right now.

You Don’t Need ‘Pervert Glasses’ to Remember Your Hike

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

A woman is holding a pair of smart glasses from Ray-Ban Meta in her hand at an eyewear store. Because these glasses allow users to take photos and videos, some outdoor and indoor swimming pools are considering banning them. (Photo: Christoph Schmidt/picture alliance via Getty Images)

In the annals of uncool accessories—the pocket protector, the cell phone holster, cargo shorts—smart glasses have already earned a place of special scorn. Unlike those utilitarian pieces, the backlash against the high-tech glasses isn’t about what they look like, though their oversized temples and chunky frames do give them a distinct Spirit Halloween look. It’s what they do: Their ability to record anyone the user looks at has led governments from Norway to New York to consider restrictions and businesses like theaters and bars to ban them. After several well-publicized incidents of users deploying smart glasses to covertly film women on the street, some detractors have labeled the wearables “pervert glasses.”

Despite that stigma, Meta, the company behind both Facebook and the most popular models of smart glasses, reportedly sold 7 million pairs of them in 2025 alone, and the eyewear has become a regular sight everywhere from bike paths to restaurants. Now, Meta wants you to take them on your next hike.

In a patent application published on October 15, Meta outlined a software update for its product that would use contextual clues like audio, speech, and data from wearables to automatically edit video captured by the glasses. The first use case the company offered: Creating a highlight reel from a recording of a hike. For example, if the glasses record the sound of a waterfall or the user saying “Look at that bird,” they could tag the footage from that moment as significant; likewise if, for example, a connected smartwatch registers your heart rate spiking. While those automatic features are still pending, hiking footage shot on the Meta glasses abounds on YouTube.

For some hikers who own them, the glasses might be a fun toy. For everyone else, they’re a privacy nightmare. While the idea of a camera monitoring your words and heart rate for hours is creepy enough, it’s not Meta Glasses’ only trick: This summer, Wired discovered code for a facial recognition system in the glasses’ software, an idea Meta had previously abandoned in 2021 over ethical concerns. The New York Times reported that the proposed feature, called Name Tag, would identify people the wearer encounters and look up information about them through Muse, the company’s AI assistant.

Exactly how much of the internet that search would have scoured is an open question: The company reportedly contemplated both limiting it to a user’s friends and allowing it to search everyone with a profile on Meta platforms like Instagram, Facebook, and Threads. While Meta quietly removed the software after Wired’s report, it’s been mum on its future plans for the technology, and third-party researchers have demonstrated how easy it is to livestream footage from the glasses into existing facial recognition engines. It’s easy to imagine a worst-case scenario where a stranger passing by could learn an uncomfortable amount about your life without ever saying a word. (While a built-in LED signals when the glasses are recording, some regulators have noted that it is not easily visible in bright sunlight, and models from many lesser-known manufacturers don’t have any kind of indicator.)

The issues only multiply from there. An investigation by two Swedish newspapers found that subcontractors hired by Meta to help train its AI regularly viewed sensitive video from its smart glasses, including clips showing nudity and users going to the bathroom. And, like any data stored in the cloud, smart glasses footage is available to any law enforcement agency that can obtain a warrant. While Meta has a decent track record of protecting users’ privacy in dealing with those demands, it doesn’t have a perfect one; according to the company’s own transparency reports, it complied with 88.8 percent of formal government data requests during one six-month period last year.

One might ask: Is any of this worth it? Meta has spent tens of millions of dollars lobbying the government not to regulate it. Is letting it ride shotgun on your hike like some real-life episode of Black Mirror a good idea? Will anyone actually look at the hundreds of thousands of hours of video these glasses capture, or will it just gather dust in a data center somewhere like most of the footage people shoot on their phones? (To be fair, I’ll also note here that smart glasses have been a game-changing tool for their small population of blind and low-vision users—about 8 percent of buyers cite accessibility as their main reason, according to a report by Security.org—though they don’t seem to be the demographic Meta is serving with these proposed software updates.) But I’m not here to judge how other hikers use tech. I’m more concerned with how it affects everyone else.

The backcountry means something different to every hiker, but to a lot of us, it’s an escape—from responsibilities, from city noise, and, increasingly, from being watched. We live today in a world that’s more monitored and recorded than ever before: Between automatic license plate readers, Ring doorbells, dash cams, and commercial security systems, an average urban dweller might feasibly spend their entire day hopping from one camera’s field of view to another. The backcountry is one of the last unsurveilled environments in North America; with the glasses’ new capabilities, Meta proposes to introduce what’s rapidly becoming a face-mounted Flock camera into it.

Whether we’re talking about smart glasses, stealth camping, or hiking with off-leash dogs, discussions about how we behave in the backcountry often devolve into arguments about individual freedom and responsibility. That misses the point: There are some behaviors that affect the experience of everyone around us. The person who decides to film their hike with a wearable that monitors everything they see and hear reaps all the benefits of that decision; it’s other backpackers that I foresee bearing the cost. Nor are those costs evenly distributed: A YouGov poll found that roughly two-thirds of people interested in buying smart glasses are men, while the Security study found that women are consistently more likely to report concern about the glasses’ cameras and potential facial recognition tech.

Someone will likely read this and ask what the big deal is; anyone with a phone, after all, can already record anyone they please. But there’s a big difference between pointing a camera at another person and being able to record anyone you look at, let alone run them through face ID. The trail is where backpackers feel the most unburdened—the least obligated to perform the roles we inhabit in our everyday lives. When anyone with a pair of shades could be recording you, living freely may start to feel a lot less sustainable.

Inside Mohamed Salah's new life after his Liverpool fall-out: LEWIS STEELE visits Turkey's 30th biggest city, where the Egyptian King hikes, drinks salted yoghurt - and still gets hero worship

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Published: 07:03 EDT, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 07:11 EDT, 6 November 2026

It started just minutes after touchdown in Turkey.

In a taxi journey across Istanbul's intercontinental bridge to Fenerbahce's stadium, the driver took a break from lighting up a cigarette or shouting profanities at other road users. He turned, looked me in the eye, and asked: 'You know Mohamed Salah?'

Two and a half hours after fighting through mind-blowing traffic (think your gridlocked motorway at 5pm multiplied by 100), on arrival at Liverpool's press conference, one gentleman did not even exchange pleasantries. He shoved a selfie stick in my face and asked about Salah.

And these two encounters were 650 miles away from where the Egyptian player now plies his trade. This fandom was only a starter when compared to the main course of Salahmania that greeted us upon arrival in the port city of Trabzon, to the east of Turkey on the coast of the Black Sea.

Here, his face and name were everywhere you looked. 'You come for Salah?' enquired the hotel receptionist. Upon hearing that was indeed the purpose of the visit, he upgraded the room to a superior suite overlooking the ocean. Result.

The streets of this city, which feels a world apart from the bustling metropolis of Istanbul, never mind Liverpool, are packed with antique dealers, and markets selling two-foot-long fish for less than £1 and the local delicacy of hamsi (anchovy to you and me).

Mohamed Salah can relax in Trabzon and has taken multiple hiking trips in the highlands (Source: Instagram/Mohamed Salah)

Fans have now taken to posing at the same spots Salah appeared at - such is their hero worship

Everywhere you turned, there was a nod to Salah. His name was heard in conversations as the melodic call to prayer rang out from the nearby Hagia Sophia mosque, his picture seemed to be hung from every building and his name was on the back of every shirt being sported in town.

Shops were selling mugs, scarves, plates, masks and even cigarette lighters shaped like Salah.

At the futuristic waterfront stadium known as Papara Park, Salahmania continued - with punters putting on impromptu barbecues and DJ sets from beside their cars similar to tailgates in American sports. Everyone in this city was dancing to the beat of the Egyptian King.

Fans, many in Trabzonspor-coloured jester hats with bells on, glowed about Salah. Most do not speak a word of English in this city not far from the Armenian and Georgian border. But those who could summed up the mood.

'We love him, he is going to make us champions,' said one.

Another told us: 'People across the country now support Trabzonspor and people come from other countries, people want us to win just because of Mohamed Salah.'

Trabzonspor supporters told Daily Mail Sport: 'We love him, he is going to make us champions'

Everywhere you turned there was a nod to Salah in his new club's kit

There were plenty of children in the crowd, and more women than expected. Some had come from afar to see him, others could not believe that Salah had chosen their team and hometown.

So, why Trabzon? Why did Salah choose this project in Turkey's 30th biggest city? Why not one of the three Istanbul giants – Fenerbahce, Galatasaray or Besiktas – who have won 28, 27 and 21 Turkish titles respectively, compared to Trabzonspor's seven, including only one since 1984.

The answers are obvious, according to sources. Salah, now 34, wanted a place where he could enjoy his football without the intense scrutiny. Every word and facial expression would generate days of news coverage in Liverpool or Istanbul or another one of Europe's giant cities.

On the outskirts of Trabzon, Salah can relax, hiking up to a group of boulders in Yayla. Even there, he's worshipped, though. Trabzonspor fans flock there to pose for photographs on the same rock where he sat for a photo posted on his Instagram.

Salah's family are settled, he is becoming obsessed with chess and he has started drinking the Turkish salted-yoghurt drink ayran each day.

On the pitch, there is a belief Trabzonspor can beat the big three and end Galatasaray's four-year grip on the title.

The team also stars former Liverpool midfielder Fabinho, ex-Manchester United goalkeeper Andre Onana and Jerome Boateng, once a stalwart at Bayern Munich. The manager Thomas Reis was poached from Ludogorets and has lost just once since his arrival.

Salah is settled with his family and faces less scrutiny than in Istanbul or Liverpool (Source: Instagram/Mohamed Salah)

Salah has scored 10 goals and assisted five more in his 15 games in Turkey so far

The highlight so far came on September 19, when Salah scored a hat-trick in a 4-0 thumping of Galatasaray. Trabzonspor have won seven league games in a row and will aim to go deep in the Conference League despite Thursday's undeserved 1-0 defeat to Freiburg.

Salah has scored 10 goals and assisted in five more in his 15 games in Turkey.

'Everyone wants to work with a star the size of Mohamed Salah,' striker Paul Onuachu, once of Southampton, told us after that game. 'He is a top guy and a world star.

'He adds so much to us and helps all of us, thanks to his experience and quality, and he contributes positively to the development of our team.

'I am grateful for him. It is a blessing to play with him.'

Salah did not have the rub of the green in the clash with the German side, who were Europa League finalists last year. But a trip to Papara Park underlined an answer to those wondering just why he picked Trabzon and not any of his other mega-money offers.

A man wearing Trabzonspor's claret and light blue colours shows off his support for Salah

A trip to Papara Park underlined an answer to those wondering just why he picked Trabzon

He had approaches from America, and the Saudi Arabian federation tried to sign him right until the day he jetted into Trabzon - where he was greeted by 35,000 fans with fireworks and flares. Besiktas held advanced talks with Salah's agent Ramy Abbas.

But Trabzonspor won the race and Salah is playing with a smile again, even though that face pulled a bit of a grimace when he spotted a familiar face or two from the English press pack waiting for him after the game.

His laugh faded into an expression that said: 'I thought I got rid of you lot pestering me!'

That face confirmed his reason for joining Trabzonspor: Salah is out of the spotlight – certainly while still in with a chance to win trophies and maintain a good level of football until next summer's Africa Cup of Nations.

After a public fall-out with Liverpool that saw the final year of his contract ripped up and him accuse the Anfield club of 'throwing him under the bus', it feels like Salah is feeling at home and happy again.

It's not difficult to turn down the multiple opportunities to buy Salah-branded magnets or shirts or teddies or socks or lighters as we bid farewell to Trabzon, but it is hard to escape the conclusion that the feeling of love is mutual in this football-mad city.

Former Rockstar Games tech director thinks GTA 6 could be coming to PC sooner than you'd expect to curb AI clones

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Former Rockstar Games North technical director Obbe Vermeij believes that the possibility of AI vibe coded ports of Grand Theft Auto 6 could push Rockstar to create an official PC version sooner than you'd expect.

Speaking to RockPaperShotgun as part of a wider interview, Vermeij said generative AI "really has changed the world.". He elaborated: "I think I would be concerned, if I was Rockstar, that somebody would just take the PS5 version and get AI to change it to PC, which won't be as good as [the developer's] effort, but it could still also hurt their sales. So, I wouldn't be surprised if there was an effort to get [the releases] a little bit closer together."

Vermeij emphasised that he doesn't know what Rockstar's plans are, but with his technical expertise, he does believe that generative AI tools are "probably" capable of pulling off a GTA 6 PC port.

"It will take a long time. It actually, physically takes hours and hours and hours to do these things," Vermeij said. "And it will take a bunch of tokens and everything, so it would probably cost like, tens of thousands of dollars in tokens. But like, you know, if it's GTA, I think people are gonna try it.".

We've already seen people thrust footage of Grand Theft Auto 6 into AI software over the past few months. AI slop videos have been pulling in millions of views on social media, and people have been 'improving' trailer footage with Nvidia's DLSS5 'yassification' machine (thanks Ars Technica). Grand Theft Auto 6 itself will not feature any generative AI or microtransactions at launch.

AI game clones continue to be a problem. Game developers have cancelled public demos in fear of having their game copied, and Steam owner Valve has gone out to say it's the developer's responsibility to prevent cloning by making their games more original. Even as Anthropic has introduced measures to prevent such practices, other AI software can still perform such tasks.
